(0)
^Y
)]
∈F_0 + G.
If
f\in\bigvee F_{0}
such that for every
n\geq 1
there is some
f_{n}\in\bigvee F_{0}
such
that
P(f_{n})
and
U\big(X;f,f_{n},\frac{1}{n}\big)
, then, for every
g\in G
, we get

U\big(X+Y;f\oplus g,f_{n}\oplus g,\frac{1}{n}\big),

and since
F_{0}+G
is a Bishop topology, by
\mathrm{BS}_{4}
we get
f\oplus g\in F_{0}+G
, hence
P(f)
.
∎

5.3
Direct spectra of Bishop spaces

As in the case of a family of Bishop spaces associated to an
I
-family of sets,
the family of Bishop spaces associated to an
(I,\preccurlyeq)
-family of sets is defined in a minimal way from
the data of
\Lambda^{\preccurlyeq}
. According to these
data, the corresponding functions
\phi_{ij}^{\preccurlyeq}
behave necessarily in a contravariant manner i.e.,

\phi_{ij}^{\Lambda^{\preccurlyeq}}\colon F_{j}\to F_{i}
. Moreover, the transport maps
\lambda_{ij}^{\preccurlyeq}
are
Bishop morphisms, and not necessarily Bishop isomorphisms.

Definition 5.3.1
.

Let
(I,\preccurlyeq)
be a directed set, and let
\Lambda^{\preccurlyeq}:=(\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1}^{\preccurlyeq}),M^{\preccurlyeq}:=(\mu_{0},\mu_{1}^{\preccurlyeq})\in\textnormal{{Fam}}(I,\preccurlyeq_{I})
.
A family of Bishop topologies associated to
\Lambda^{\preccurlyeq}
is a pair
\Phi^{\Lambda^{\preccurlyeq}}:=\big(\phi_{0}^{\Lambda^{\preccurlyeq}},\phi_{1}^{\Lambda^{\preccurlyeq}}\big)
, where
\phi_{0}^{\Lambda^{\preccurlyeq}}:I\rightsquigarrow\mathbb{V}_{0}

and
\phi_{1}^{\Lambda^{\preccurlyeq}}:\bigcurlywedge_{(i,j)\in\preccurlyeq(I)}\mathbb{F}\big(\phi_{0}^{\Lambda^{\preccurlyeq}}(j),\phi_{0}^{\Lambda^{\preccurlyeq}}(i)\big)
,
such that the following conditions hold:

(i)

\phi_{0}^{\Lambda^{\preccurlyeq}}(i):=F_{i}\subseteq\mathbb{F}(\lambda_{0}(i))
,
and
\mathcal{F}_{i}:=(\lambda_{0}(i),F_{i})
is a Bishop space, for every
i\in I
.

(ii)

\lambda_{ij}^{\preccurlyeq}\in\mathrm{Mor}(\mathcal{F}_{i},\mathcal{F}_{j})
, for every
(i,j)\in D^{\preccurlyeq}(I)
.

(iii)

\phi_{1}^{\Lambda^{\preccurlyeq}}(i,j):=\big(\lambda_{ij}^{\preccurlyeq}\big)^{*}
,
for every
(i,j)\in D^{\preccurlyeq}(I)
, where, if
f\in F_{j}
,

\big(\lambda_{ij}^{\preccurlyeq}\big)^{*}(f):=f\circ\lambda_{ij}^{\preccurlyeq}
.

The structure
S(\Lambda^{\preccurlyeq}):=(\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1}^{\preccurlyeq},\phi_{0}^{\Lambda^{\preccurlyeq}},\phi_{1}^{\Lambda^{\preccurlyeq}})

is called a direct spectrum over
(I,\preccurlyeq)
, or an

(I,\preccurlyeq)
-spectrum with Bishop spaces
(\mathcal{F}_{i})_{i\in I}
and Bishop morphisms

(\lambda_{ij}^{\preccurlyeq})_{(i,j)\in D^{\preccurlyeq}(I)}
. If

S(M^{\preccurlyeq}):=(\mu_{0},\mu_{1},\phi_{0}^{M^{\preccurlyeq}},\phi_{1}^{M^{\preccurlyeq}})
is an
(I,\preccurlyeq)
-spectrum
with Bishop spaces
(\mathcal{G}_{i})_{i\in I}
and Bishop morphisms
(\mu_{ij}^{\preccurlyeq})_{(i,j)\in D^{\preccurlyeq}(I)}
,
a direct spectrum-map
\Psi
from
S(\Lambda^{\preccurlyeq})
to
S(M^{\preccurlyeq})
,
in symbols
\Psi\colon S(\Lambda^{\preccurlyeq})\Rightarrow S(M^{\preccurlyeq})
,
is a direct family-map
\Psi:\Lambda^{\preccurlyeq}\Rightarrow M^{\preccurlyeq}
. The totality of direct spectrum-maps
from
S(\Lambda^{\preccurlyeq})
to
S(M^{\preccurlyeq})
is denoted by
\textnormal{{Map}}_{(I,\preccurlyeq_{I})}(S(\Lambda^{\preccurlyeq}),S(M^{\preccurlyeq}))
and it is equipped with the
equality of
\textnormal{{Map}}_{I}(\Lambda^{\preccurlyeq},M^{\preccurlyeq})
.
A direct spectrum-map
\Psi:S(\Lambda^{\preccurlyeq})\Rightarrow S(M^{\preccurlyeq})

is called continuous, if

\forall_{i\in I}\big(\Psi_{i}\in\mathrm{Mor}(\mathcal{F}_{i},\mathcal{G}_{i})\big)
, and let
\textnormal{{Cont}}_{(I,\preccurlyeq_{I})}(S(\Lambda^{\preccurlyeq}),S(M^{\preccurlyeq}))

be their totality, equipped with the equality of
\textnormal{{Map}}_{I}(\Lambda^{\preccurlyeq},M^{\preccurlyeq})
. The
totality
\textnormal{{Spec}}(I,\preccurlyeq_{I})
of direct spectra over
(I,\preccurlyeq_{I})
is equipped with an
equality defined similarly to the equality on
\textnormal{{Spec}}(I)
.
A contravariant direct spectrum

S(\Lambda^{\succcurlyeq}):=(\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1}^{\succcurlyeq};\phi_{0}^{\Lambda^{\succcurlyeq}},\phi_{1}^{\Lambda^{\succcurlyeq}})

over
(I,\preccurlyeq)
, a contravariant direct spectrum-map
\Psi:S(\Lambda^{\succcurlyeq})\Rightarrow S(M^{\succcurlyeq})
, and
their totalities

\textnormal{{Map}}_{(I,\preccurlyeq_{I})}(S(\Lambda^{\succcurlyeq}),S(M^{\succcurlyeq}))
,

\textnormal{{Spec}}(I,\succcurlyeq_{I})
are defined similarly.

Remark 5.3.2
.

Let
(I,\preccurlyeq)
be a directed set,

S(\Lambda^{\preccurlyeq}):=(\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1};\phi_{0}^{\Lambda^{\preccurlyeq}},\phi_{1}^{\Lambda^{\preccurlyeq}})\in\textnormal{{Spec}}(I,\preccurlyeq_{I})

with Bishop spaces
(\mathcal{F}_{i})_{i\in I}
and Bishop morphisms

(\lambda_{ij}^{\preccurlyeq})_{(i,j)\in D^{\preccurlyeq}(I)}
,

S(M^{\preccurlyeq}):=(\mu_{0},\mu_{1},\phi_{0}^{M^{\preccurlyeq}},\phi_{1}^{M^{\preccurlyeq}})\in\textnormal{{Spec}}(I,\preccurlyeq_{I})

with Bishop spaces
(\mathcal{G}_{i})_{i\in I}
and Bishop morphisms
(\mu_{ij}^{\preccurlyeq})_{(i,j)\in D^{\preccurlyeq}(I)}
,
and
\Psi\colon S(\Lambda^{\preccurlyeq})\Rightarrow S(M^{\preccurlyeq})
. Then
\Phi^{\Lambda^{\preccurlyeq}}:=\big(\phi_{0}^{\Lambda^{\preccurlyeq}},\phi_{1}^{\Lambda^{\preccurlyeq}}\big)
is an
(I,\succcurlyeq)
-family of sets,
and if
\Psi
is continuous, then, for every
(i,j)\in D^{\preccurlyeq}(I)
,
the following diagram commutes
F_{j}
F_{i}
.
{{G_{i}}}
G_{j}
\big(\lambda_{ij}^{\preccurlyeq}\big)^{*}
\big(\mu_{ij}^{\preccurlyeq}\big)^{*}
\big(\Psi_{j}\big)^{*}
\big(\Psi_{i}\big)^{*}

Proof.

Since
(\lambda_{ii}^{\preccurlyeq})^{*}(f):=f\circ\lambda_{ii}^{\preccurlyeq}:=f\circ\mathrm{id}_{\lambda_{0}(i)}:=f
, for every

f\in F_{i}
, we get
(\lambda_{ii}^{\preccurlyeq})^{*}:=\mathrm{id}_{F_{i}}
. If
i\preccurlyeq j\preccurlyeq k
and
f\in F_{k}
,
the required commutativity of the following diagram is shown:
F_{j}
F_{k}
F_{i}
\big(\lambda_{jk}^{\preccurlyeq}\big)^{*}
\big(\lambda_{ij}^{\preccurlyeq}\big)^{*}
\ \big(\lambda_{ik}^{\preccurlyeq}\big)^{*}

\big(\lambda_{ij}^{\preccurlyeq}\big)^{*}\big(\big(\lambda_{jk}^{\preccurlyeq}\big)^{*}(f)\big):=\big(\lambda_{ij}^{\preccurlyeq}\big)^{*}(f\circ\lambda_{jk}^{\preccurlyeq}):=(f\circ\lambda_{jk}^{\preccurlyeq})\circ\lambda_{ij}^{\preccurlyeq}:=f\circ(\lambda_{jk}^{\preccurlyeq}\circ\lambda_{ij}^{\preccurlyeq})=f\circ\lambda_{ik}^{\preccurlyeq}:=\big(\lambda_{ik}\big)^{*}(f).

To show the required commutativity, if
g\in G_{j}
, then

\big(\lambda_{ij}^{\preccurlyeq}\big)^{*}\big(\big(\Psi_{j}^{\preccurlyeq}\big)^{*}(g)\big):=\big(\Psi_{j}\big)^{*}(g)\circ\lambda_{ij}^{\preccurlyeq}:=\big(g\circ\Psi_{j}\big)\circ\lambda_{ij}^{\prec}=_{F_{i}}g\circ\big(\Psi_{j}\circ\lambda_{ij}^{\preccurlyeq}\big)=_{F_{i}}g\circ\big(\mu_{ij}^{\preccurlyeq}\circ\Psi_{i}\big)

=_{F_{i}}\big(g\circ\mu_{ij}^{\preccurlyeq}\big)\circ\Psi_{i}:=\big(\Psi_{i}\big)^{*}\big(g\circ\mu_{ij}^{\preccurlyeq}\big):=\big(\Psi_{i}\big)^{*}\big(\big(\mu_{ij}^{\preccurlyeq}\big)^{*}(g)\big).\qed

5.4
The topology on the
\sum
-set of a direct spectrum

Remark 5.4.1
.

Let
(I,\preccurlyeq)
be a directed set and

S(\Lambda^{\preccurlyeq}):=(\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1},\phi_{0}^{\Lambda^{\preccurlyeq}},\phi_{1}^{\Lambda^{\preccurlyeq}})\in\textnormal{{Spec}}(I,D^{\preccurlyeq}(I))

with Bishop spaces
(\mathcal{F}_{i})_{i\in I}
and Bishop morphisms

(\lambda_{ij}^{\preccurlyeq})_{(i,j)\in\ D^{\preccurlyeq}(I)}
. If
\Theta\in\prod_{i\in I}^{\succcurlyeq}F_{i}
,
the following operation is a function

f_{\Theta}:\bigg(\sum_{i\in I}^{\preccurlyeq}\lambda_{0}(i)\bigg)\rightsquigarrow{\mathbb{R}},\ \ \ \ f_{\Theta}(i,x):=\Theta_{i}(x),\ \ \ \ (i,x)\in\sum_{i\in I}^{\preccurlyeq}\lambda_{0}(i).

Proof.

Let
(i,x)=_{{\sum_{i\in I}^{\preccurlyeq}\lambda_{0}(i)}}(j,y):\Leftrightarrow\exists_{k\succcurlyeq i,j}\big(\lambda_{ik}^{\preccurlyeq}(x)=_{{\lambda_{0}(k)}}\lambda_{jk}^{\preccurlyeq}(y)\big)
. Since

\Theta_{i}=\phi_{ki}^{\succcurlyeq}(\Theta_{k}):=(\lambda_{ik}^{\preccurlyeq})^{*}(\Theta_{k}):=\Theta_{k}\circ\lambda_{ik}^{\preccurlyeq},

and similarly
\Theta_{j}=\Theta_{k}\circ\lambda_{jk}^{\preccurlyeq}
, we have that

\Theta_{i}(x)=\big[\Theta_{k}\circ\lambda_{ik}^{\preccurlyeq}\big](x):=\Theta_{k}\big(\lambda_{ik}^{\preccurlyeq}(x)\big)=\Theta_{k}\big(\lambda_{jk}^{\preccurlyeq}(y)\big):=\big[\Theta_{k}\circ\lambda_{jk}^{\preccurlyeq}\big](y)=\Theta_{j}(y).\qed

Definition 5.4.2
.

Let
(I,\preccurlyeq)
be a directed set and

S(\Lambda^{\preccurlyeq}):=(\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1},\phi_{0}^{\Lambda^{\preccurlyeq}},\phi_{1}^{\Lambda^{\preccurlyeq}})\in\textnormal{{Spec}}(I,\preccurlyeq_{I})

with Bishop spaces
(\mathcal{F}_{i})_{i\in I}
and Bishop morphisms

(\lambda_{ij}^{\preccurlyeq})_{(i,j)\in D^{\preccurlyeq}(I)}
.
The Bishop space

\sum_{i\in I}^{\preccurlyeq}\mathcal{F}_{i}:=\bigg(\sum_{i\in I}^{\preccurlyeq}\lambda_{0}(i),\int_{i\in I}^{\preccurlyeq}F_{i}\bigg)\ \ \ \ \mbox{where}\ \ \int_{i\in I}^{\preccurlyeq}F_{i}:=\bigvee_{{\Theta\in\prod_{i\in I}^{\succcurlyeq}F_{i}}}f_{\Theta},

is the sum Bishop space of
S(\Lambda^{\preccurlyeq})
. If
S^{\succcurlyeq}
is
a contravariant direct spectrum over
(I,\preccurlyeq)
, the sum Bishop space of
S(\Lambda^{\succcurlyeq})
is defined
dually.

Lemma 5.4.3
.

Let
S(\Lambda^{\preccurlyeq}):=(\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1}^{\preccurlyeq},\phi_{0}^{\Lambda^{\preccurlyeq}},\phi_{1}^{\Lambda^{\preccurlyeq}}),S(M^{\preccurlyeq}):=(\mu_{0},\mu_{1}^{\preccurlyeq},\phi_{0}^{M^{\preccurlyeq}},\phi_{1}^{M^{\preccurlyeq}})\in\textnormal{{Spec}}(I,\preccurlyeq_{I})
,
and let
\Psi:S(\Lambda^{\preccurlyeq})\to S(M^{\preccurlyeq})
be continuous.
If
H\in\prod_{i\in I}^{\succcurlyeq}G_{i}
, the dependent operation

H^{*}:\bigcurlywedge_{i\in I}F_{i}
, defined by
H^{*}_{i}:=\Psi^{*}_{i}(H_{i}):=H_{i}\circ\Psi_{i}
, for every
i\in I
,
is in
\prod_{i\in I}^{\succcurlyeq}F_{i}
.

Proof.

If
i\preccurlyeq j
, we need to show that
H_{i}^{*}=(\lambda_{ij}^{\preccurlyeq})^{*}(H_{j}^{*})=H_{j}^{*}\circ\lambda_{ij}^{\preccurlyeq}
. Since

H\in\prod_{i\in I}^{\succcurlyeq}G_{i}
, we have that
H_{i}=H_{j}\circ\mu_{ij}^{\preccurlyeq}
, and by the continuity of
\Psi

and the commutativity of the diagram
\mu_{0}(i)
\mu_{0}(j)
,
\lambda_{0}(j)
\lambda_{0}(i)
\mu_{ij}^{\preccurlyeq}
\lambda_{ij}^{\preccurlyeq}
\Psi_{i}
\Psi_{j}

H_{j}^{*}\circ\lambda_{ij}^{\preccurlyeq}:=\Psi_{j}^{*}(H_{j})\circ\lambda_{ij}^{\preccurlyeq}:=\big(H_{j}\circ\Psi_{j}\big)\circ\lambda_{ij}^{\preccurlyeq}:=H_{j}(0)\circ\big(\Psi_{j}\circ\lambda_{ij}^{\preccurlyeq}\big)

=H_{j}\circ\big(\mu_{ij}^{\preccurlyeq}\circ\Psi_{i}\big)=\big(H_{j}\circ\big(\mu_{ij}^{\preccurlyeq}\big)\circ\Psi_{i}=H_{i}\circ\Psi_{i}:=\Psi^{*}_{i}(H_{i}):=H^{*}_{i}.\qed

Proposition 5.4.4
.

Let
S(\Lambda^{\preccurlyeq}):=(\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1}^{\preccurlyeq},\phi_{0}^{\Lambda^{\preccurlyeq}},\phi_{1}^{\Lambda^{\preccurlyeq}})
and

S(M^{\preccurlyeq}):=(\mu_{0},\mu_{1}^{\preccurlyeq},\phi_{0}^{M^{\preccurlyeq}},\phi_{1}^{M^{\preccurlyeq}})
be spectra over
(I,\preccurlyeq_{I})
,
and let
\Psi:S(\Lambda^{\preccurlyeq})\Rightarrow S(M^{\preccurlyeq})
.

(i)

If
i\in I
, then
e_{i}^{\Lambda^{\preccurlyeq}}\in\mathrm{Mor}\big(\mathcal{F}_{i},\sum_{i\in I}^{\preccurlyeq}\mathcal{F}_{i}\big)
.

(ii)

If
\Psi
is continuous, then

\Sigma^{\preccurlyeq}\Psi\in\mathrm{Mor}\big(\sum_{i\in I}^{\preccurlyeq}\mathcal{F}_{i},\sum_{i\in I}^{\preccurlyeq}\mathcal{G}_{i}\big)
.

Proof.

(i) By the
\bigvee
-lifting of morphisms it suffices to show that

\forall_{\Theta\in\prod_{i\in I}^{\succcurlyeq}F_{i}}\big(f_{\Theta}\circ e_{i}^{\Lambda^{\preccurlyeq}}\in F_{i}\big)
.
If
x\in\lambda_{0}(i)
, then

\big(f_{\Theta}\circ e_{i}^{\Lambda^{\preccurlyeq}}\big)(x):=f_{\Theta}(i,x):=\Theta_{i}(x),
hence

f_{\Theta}\circ e_{i}^{\Lambda^{\preccurlyeq}}:=\Theta_{i}\in F_{i}
.

(ii) By the
\bigvee
-lifting of morphisms it suffices to show that

\forall_{H\in\prod_{i\in I}^{\succcurlyeq}G_{i}}\bigg(g_{H}\circ\Sigma^{\preccurlyeq}\Psi\in\int_{i\in I}^{\preccurlyeq}F_{i}\bigg).

If
i\in I
and
x\in\lambda_{0}(i)
, and if
H^{*}\in\prod_{i\in I}^{\succcurlyeq}F_{i}
, defined
in Lemma
5.4.3
, then

\big(g_{H}\circ\Sigma^{\preccurlyeq}\Psi\big)(i,x):=g_{H}(i,\Psi_{i}(x)):=H_{i}(\Psi_{i}(x)):=(H_{i}\circ\Psi_{i})(x):=f_{H^{*}}(i,x),

and
g_{H}\circ\Sigma^{\preccurlyeq}\Psi:=f_{H^{*}}\in\int_{i\in I}^{\preccurlyeq}F_{i}
.
∎

5.5
Direct limit of a covariant spectrum of Bishop spaces

If
X
is a set, by Corollary
4.7.3
the family
\textnormal{{Eql}}(X):=\big(\textnormal{{eql}}_{0}^{X},\mathcal{E}^{X},\textnormal{{eql}}_{1}^{X}\big)\in\textnormal{{Set}}(X,X)
,
where
\textnormal{{eql}}_{0}^{X}(x):=\{y\in X\mid y=_{X}x\}
. Consequently, if
f\colon X\to Y
, there is unique

\textnormal{{eql}}_{0}f\colon\textnormal{{eql}}_{0}X(X)\to Y
such that the following diagram commutes
\textnormal{{eql}}_{0}X(X)
X
Y,
\ \textnormal{{eql}}_{0}f
\textnormal{{eql}}^{*}_{0}
f

where
\textnormal{{eql}}_{0}X(X)
is the totality
X
with the equality
x=_{\textnormal{{eql}}_{0}X(X)}x{{}^{\prime}}:\Leftrightarrow\textnormal{{eql}}_{0}^{X}(x)=_{\mathcal{P}(X)}\textnormal{{eql}}_{0}^{X}(x{{}^{\prime}})
. As
\textnormal{{Eql}}(X)\in\textnormal{{Set}}(X,X)
, we get
\textnormal{{eql}}_{0}^{X}(x)=_{\mathcal{P}(X)}\textnormal{{eql}}_{0}^{X}(x{{}^{\prime}})\Leftrightarrow x=_{X}x{{}^{\prime}}
.
The map
\textnormal{{eql}}_{0}^{*}\colon X\to\textnormal{{eql}}_{0}X(X)
is defined by the identity map-rule, written in the
form
x\mapsto\textnormal{{eql}}_{0}^{X}(x)
, for every
x\in X
. We use the set
\textnormal{{eql}}_{0}X(X)
to define the direct
limit of a direct spectrum of Bishop spaces. In what follows we avoid including the superscript
X
in our notation.

Definition 5.5.1
.

Let
S(\Lambda^{\preccurlyeq}):=(\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1}^{\preccurlyeq},\phi_{0}^{\Lambda^{\preccurlyeq}},\phi_{1}^{\Lambda^{\preccurlyeq}})\in\textnormal{{Spec}}(I,\preccurlyeq_{I})
and

\textnormal{{eql}}_{0}\colon\sum_{i\in I}^{\preccurlyeq}\lambda_{0}(i)\rightsquigarrow\mathbb{V}_{0}
, defined by

\textnormal{{eql}}_{0}(i,x):=\bigg\{(j,y)\in\sum_{i\in I}^{\preccurlyeq}\lambda_{0}(i)\mid(j,y)=_{{\sum_{i\in I}^{\preccurlyeq}\lambda_{0}(i)}}(i,x)\bigg\};\ \ \ \ (i,x)\in\sum_{i\in I}^{\preccurlyeq}\lambda_{0}(i),.

The direct limit
\underset{\to}{\mathrm{Lim}}\lambda_{0}(i)

of
S(\Lambda^{\preccurlyeq})
is the set

\underset{\to}{\mathrm{Lim}}\lambda_{0}(i):=\textnormal{{eql}}_{0}\sum_{i\in I}^{\preccurlyeq}\lambda_{0}(i)\bigg(\sum_{i\in I}^{\preccurlyeq}\lambda_{0}(i)\bigg),

\textnormal{{eql}}_{0}(i,x)=_{\underset{\to}{\mathrm{Lim}}\lambda_{0}(i)}\textnormal{{eql}}_{0}(j,y):\Leftrightarrow\textnormal{{eql}}_{0}(i,x)=_{\mathcal{P}\big(\sum_{i\in I}^{\preccurlyeq}\lambda_{0}(i)\big)}\textnormal{{eql}}_{0}(j,y)\Leftrightarrow(i,x)=_{\sum_{i\in I}^{\preccurlyeq}\lambda_{0}(i)}(j,y).

We write
\textnormal{{eql}}_{0}^{\Lambda^{\preccurlyeq}}
when we need to express
the dependence of
\textnormal{{eql}}_{0}
from
\Lambda^{\preccurlyeq}
.

Remark 5.5.2
.

If
S(\Lambda^{\preccurlyeq}):=(\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1}^{\preccurlyeq},\phi_{0}^{\Lambda^{\preccurlyeq}},\phi_{1}^{\Lambda^{\preccurlyeq}})\in\textnormal{{Spec}}(I,\preccurlyeq_{I})
and
i\in I
, the operation

\textnormal{{eql}}_{i}\colon\lambda_{0}(i)\rightsquigarrow\underset{\to}{\mathrm{Lim}}\lambda_{0}(i)
, defined by
\textnormal{{eql}}_{i}(x):=\textnormal{{eql}}_{0}(i,x)
, for every
x\in\lambda_{0}(i)
,
is a function.

Proof.

If
x,x{{}^{\prime}}\in\lambda_{0}(i)
such that
x=_{\lambda_{0}(i)}x{{}^{\prime}}
, then

\displaystyle\textnormal{{eql}}_{i}(x)=_{\underset{\to}{\mathrm{Lim}}\lambda_{0}(i)}\textnormal{{eql}}_{i}(x{{}^{\prime}})

\displaystyle:\Leftrightarrow\textnormal{{eql}}_{0}(i,x)=_{\underset{\to}{\mathrm{Lim}}\lambda_{0}(i)}\textnormal{{eql}}_{0}(i,x{{}^{\prime}})

\displaystyle\Leftrightarrow(i,x)=_{\sum_{i\in I}^{\preccurlyeq}\lambda_{0}(i)}(i,x{{}^{\prime}})

\displaystyle:\Leftrightarrow\exists_{k\in I}\big(i\preccurlyeq k\ \&\ \lambda_{ik}^{\preccurlyeq}(x)=_{\lambda_{0}(k)}\lambda_{ik}^{\preccurlyeq}(x{{}^{\prime}})\big),

which holds, since
\lambda_{ik}^{\preccurlyeq}
is a function, and hence
if
x=_{\lambda_{0}(i)}x{{}^{\prime}}
, then

\lambda_{ik}^{\preccurlyeq}(x)=_{\lambda_{0}(k)}\lambda_{ik}^{\preccurlyeq}(x{{}^{\prime}})
, for every
k\in I
such that

i\preccurlyeq k
. Such a
k\in I
always exists e.g., one can take
k:=i
.
∎

Definition 5.5.3
.

Let
S(\Lambda^{\preccurlyeq}):=(\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1}^{\preccurlyeq},\phi_{0}^{\Lambda^{\preccurlyeq}},\phi_{1}^{\Lambda^{\preccurlyeq}})\in\textnormal{{Spec}}(I,\preccurlyeq_{I})
with Bishop spaces
(F_{i})_{i\in I}
and Bishop morphisms

(\lambda_{ij}^{\preccurlyeq})_{(i,j)\in D^{\preccurlyeq}(I)}
.
The direct limit of
S(\Lambda^{\preccurlyeq})

is the Bishop space

\underset{\to}{\mathrm{Lim}}\mathcal{F}_{i}:=\big(\underset{\to}{\mathrm{Lim}}\lambda_{0}(i),\underset{\to}{\mathrm{Lim}}F_{i}\big),\ \ \ \ \mbox{where}

\underset{\to}{\mathrm{Lim}}F_{i}:=\bigvee_{{\Theta\in\prod_{i\in I}^{\succcurlyeq}F_{i}}}\textnormal{{eql}}_{0}f_{\Theta},

\textnormal{{eql}}_{0}f_{\Theta}\big(\textnormal{{eql}}_{0}(i,x)\big):=f_{\Theta}(i,x):=\Theta_{i}(x);\ \ \ \ \textnormal{{eql}}_{0}(i,x)\in\underset{\to}{\mathrm{Lim}}\lambda_{0}(i)

\underset{\to}{\mathrm{Lim}}\lambda_{0}(i)
\sum_{i\in I}^{\preccurlyeq}\lambda_{0}(i)
{\mathbb{R}}
.
\ \textnormal{{eql}}_{0}f_{\Theta}
\textnormal{{eql}}^{*}_{0}
f_{\Theta}

Remark 5.5.4
.

If
(I,\preccurlyeq)
is a directed set,
\mathcal{G}:=(Y,G)
is a Bishop space, and

S(\Lambda^{\preccurlyeq,Y})

is the constant direct spectrum over
(I,\preccurlyeq_{I})
with Bishop space
\mathcal{G}
and Bishop morphism
\mathrm{id}_{Y}
, the direct limit

\underset{\to}{\mathrm{Lim}}\mathcal{G}
of
S(\Lambda^{\preccurlyeq,Y})

is Bishop-isomorphic to
\mathcal{G}
. Moreover, every Bishop
space is Bishop-isomorphic to the direct limit of a direct spectrum over any given directed set.

Proof.

The proof is straightforward.
∎

Proposition 5.5.5

(Universal property of the direct limit)
.

If
S(\Lambda^{\preccurlyeq}):=(\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1}^{\preccurlyeq},\phi_{0}^{\Lambda^{\preccurlyeq}},\phi_{1}^{\Lambda^{\preccurlyeq}})\in\textnormal{{Spec}}(I,\preccurlyeq_{I})

with Bishop spaces
(\mathcal{F}_{i})_{i\in I}
and Bishop morphisms
(\lambda_{ij}^{\preccurlyeq})_{(i,j)\in\preccurlyeq(I)}
,
its direct limit
\underset{\to}{\mathrm{Lim}}\mathcal{F}_{i}
satisfies the universal property of
direct limits i.e.,

(i)

For every
i\in I
, we have that
\textnormal{{eql}}_{i}\in\mathrm{Mor}(\mathcal{F}_{i},\underset{\to}{\mathrm{Lim}}\mathcal{F}_{i})
.

(ii)

If
i\preccurlyeq_{I}j
, the following left diagram commutes
\underset{\to}{\mathrm{Lim}}\lambda_{0}(i)
\lambda_{0}(j)
\lambda_{0}(i)
\lambda_{0}(i)
\lambda_{0}(j)
.
Y
\ \textnormal{{eql}}_{j}
\textnormal{{eql}}_{i}\
\lambda_{ij}^{\preccurlyeq}
\ \varepsilon_{j}
\varepsilon_{i}\
\lambda_{ij}^{\preccurlyeq}

(iii)

If
\mathcal{G}:=(Y,G)
is a Bishop space and

\varepsilon_{i}:\lambda_{0}(i)\to Y\in\mathrm{Mor}(\mathcal{F}_{i},\mathcal{G})
, for every
i\in I
, such that if

i\preccurlyeq j
, the
above right diagram commutes,
there is a unique function

h\colon\underset{\to}{\mathrm{Lim}}\lambda_{0}(i)\to Y\in\mathrm{Mor}(\underset{\to}{\mathrm{Lim}}\mathcal{F}_{i},\mathcal{G})
such that
the following diagrams commute
Y
\lambda_{0}(j)
,
\lambda_{0}(i)
\underset{\to}{\mathrm{Lim}}\lambda_{0}(i)
.
\ \ \varepsilon_{j}
\varepsilon_{i}\
\lambda_{ij}^{\preccurlyeq}\ \ \ \ \ \
\ \textnormal{{eql}}_{j}
\textnormal{{eql}}_{i}\
\ \ \ h

Proof.

For the proof of (i), we use the
\bigvee
-lifting of morphisms. We have that

\textnormal{{eql}}_{i}\in\mathrm{Mor}(\mathcal{F}_{i},\underset{\to}{\mathrm{Lim}}\mathcal{F}_{i})\Leftrightarrow\forall_{\Theta\in\prod_{i\in I}^{\preccurlyeq}F_{i}}\big(\textnormal{{eql}}_{0}f_{\Theta}\circ\textnormal{{eql}}_{i}\in F_{i}\big).

If
x\in\lambda_{0}(i)
, then

\big(\textnormal{{eql}}_{0}f_{\Theta}\circ\textnormal{{eql}}_{i}\big)(x):=\textnormal{{eql}}_{0}f_{\Theta}\big(\textnormal{{eql}}_{0}(i,x)\big):=f_{\Theta}(i,x):=\Theta_{i}(x)

hence
\textnormal{{eql}}_{0}f_{\Theta}\circ\textnormal{{eql}}_{i}:=\Theta_{i}\in F_{i}
. For the proof of (ii), if
x\in\lambda_{0}(i)
, then

\displaystyle\textnormal{{eql}}_{j}(\lambda_{ij}^{\preccurlyeq}(x))=_{\underset{\to}{\mathrm{Lim}}\lambda_{0}(i)}\textnormal{{eql}}_{i}(x)

\displaystyle:\Leftrightarrow\textnormal{{eql}}_{0}\big(j,\lambda_{ij}^{\preccurlyeq}(x)\big)=_{\underset{\to}{\mathrm{Lim}}\lambda_{0}(i)}\textnormal{{eql}}_{0}(i,x)

\displaystyle\Leftrightarrow\big(j,\lambda_{ij}^{\preccurlyeq}(x)\big)=_{\sum_{i\in I}^{\preccurlyeq}\lambda_{0}(i)}(i,x)

\displaystyle:\Leftrightarrow\exists_{k\in I}\big(i\preccurlyeq k\ \&\ j\preccurlyeq k\ \&\ \lambda_{ik}^{\preccurlyeq}(x)=_{\lambda_{0}(k)}\lambda_{jk}^{\preccurlyeq}(\lambda_{ij}^{\preccurlyeq}(x))\big),

which holds, since if
k\in I
with
j\preccurlyeq k
, the equality

\lambda_{ik}^{\preccurlyeq}(x)=_{\lambda_{0}(k)}\lambda_{jk}^{\preccurlyeq}(\lambda_{ij}^{\preccurlyeq}(x))
holds by
the definition of a direct family of sets, and by the definition of a directed set such a
k
always exists. To prove
(iii) let the operation
h\colon\underset{\to}{\mathrm{Lim}}\lambda_{0}(i)\rightsquigarrow Y
, defined by

h\big(\textnormal{{eql}}_{0}(i,x)\big):=\varepsilon_{i}(x)
, for every
\omega(i,x)\in\underset{\to}{\mathrm{Lim}}\lambda_{0}(i)
.
First we show that
h
is a function. Let

eql
_0 (i, x) =_

→

Lim

λ
_0 (i)
eql
_0 (j, y) ⇔∃_k ∈I
(
i, j ≼k &
λ
_ik^≼(x) =_
λ
_0(k)

λ
_jk^≼(y)
)
.
By the supposed commutativity of the following diagrams
Y
\lambda_{0}(k)
\lambda_{0}(i)
\lambda_{0}(j)
\lambda_{0}(k)
Y
\ \varepsilon_{k}
\varepsilon_{i}\
\lambda_{ik}^{\preccurlyeq}
\ \varepsilon_{k}
\varepsilon_{j}\
\lambda_{jk}^{\preccurlyeq}

we get

h\big(\omega(i,x)\big):=\varepsilon_{i}(x)=\varepsilon_{k}\big(\lambda_{ik}^{\preccurlyeq}(x)\big)=\varepsilon_{k}\big(\lambda_{jk}^{\preccurlyeq}(y)\big)=\varepsilon_{j}(y):=h\big(\omega(j,y)\big)
.
Next we show that
h
is a Bishop morphism. By the
\bigvee
-lifting of morphisms we have that

h\in\mathrm{Mor}(\underset{\to}{\mathrm{Lim}}\mathcal{F}_{i},\mathcal{G})\Leftrightarrow\forall_{g\in G}\big(g\circ h\in\underset{\to}{\mathrm{Lim}}F_{i}\big)
.
If
g\in G
, we show that the dependent operation

\Theta_{g}:\bigcurlywedge_{i\in I}F_{i}
, defined by
\Theta_{g}(i):=g\circ\varepsilon_{i}
, for every
i\in I
,
is well-defined, since
\varepsilon_{i}\in\mathrm{Mor}(\mathcal{F}_{i},\mathcal{G})
, and
\Theta_{g}\in\prod_{i\in I}^{\succcurlyeq}F_{i}
.
To prove the latter, if
i\preccurlyeq k
, we show that
\Theta_{g}(i)=\Theta_{g}(k)\circ\lambda_{ik}^{\preccurlyeq}
. By the commutativity of the above left diagram we have that

\Theta_{g}(k)\circ\lambda_{ik}^{\preccurlyeq}:=(g\circ\varepsilon_{k})\circ\lambda_{ik}^{\preccurlyeq}:=g\circ(\varepsilon_{k}\circ\lambda_{ik}^{\preccurlyeq})=g\circ\varepsilon_{i}:=\Theta_{g}(i)
,
Hence
f_{\Theta_{g}}\in\underset{\to}{\mathrm{Lim}}F_{i}
. Since

(g\circ h)\big(\textnormal{{eql}}_{0}(i,x)\big):=g(\varepsilon_{i}(x)):=(g\circ\varepsilon_{i})(x):=[\Theta_{g}(i)](x):=f_{\Theta_{g}}\big((\textnormal{{eql}}_{0}(i,x)\big)
,
we get
g\circ h:=f_{\Theta_{g}}\in\underset{\to}{\mathrm{Lim}}F_{i}
. The uniqueness of
h
, and the commutativity
of the diagram in property (iii) follow immediately.
∎

The uniqueness of
\underset{\to}{\mathrm{Lim}}\lambda_{0}(i)
, up to Bishop isomorphism, is shown easily from
its universal property.
Note that if
i,j\in I
,
x\in\lambda_{0}(i)
and
y\in\lambda_{0}(j)
, we have that

\displaystyle\textnormal{{eql}}_{i}(x)=_{\underset{\to}{\mathrm{Lim}}\lambda_{0}(i)}\textnormal{{eql}}_{j}(y)

\displaystyle:\Leftrightarrow\textnormal{{eql}}_{0}(i,x)=_{\underset{\to}{\mathrm{Lim}}\lambda_{0}(i)}\textnormal{{eql}}_{0}(j,y)

\displaystyle\Leftrightarrow(i,x)=_{\sum_{i\in I}^{\preccurlyeq}\lambda_{0}(i)}(j,y)

\displaystyle:\Leftrightarrow\exists_{k\in I}\big(i\preccurlyeq k\ \&\ j\preccurlyeq k\ \&\ \lambda_{ik}^{\preccurlyeq}(x)=_{\lambda_{0}(k)}\lambda_{jk}^{\preccurlyeq}(y)\big).

Definition 5.5.6
.

Let
S^{\preccurlyeq}:=(\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1}^{\preccurlyeq};\phi_{0}^{\Lambda^{\preccurlyeq}},\phi_{1}^{\Lambda^{\preccurlyeq}})
be
a direct spectrum over
(I,\preccurlyeq)
.
If
i\in I
, an element
x
of
\lambda_{0}(i)
is a
representative of
\omega(z)\in\underset{\to}{\mathrm{Lim}}\lambda_{0}(i)
,
if
\omega_{i}(x)=_{{\underset{\to}{\mathrm{Lim}}\lambda_{0}(i)}}\omega(z)
.

Although an element
\textnormal{{eql}}_{0}(z)\in\underset{\to}{\mathrm{Lim}}\lambda_{0}(i)
may not have a representative
in every
\lambda_{0}(i)
, it surely has one at some
\lambda_{0}(i)
. Actually, the following holds.

Proposition 5.5.7
.

For every
n\geq 1
and every
\textnormal{{eql}}_{0}(z_{1}),\ldots,\textnormal{{eql}}_{0}(z_{n})\in\underset{\to}{\mathrm{Lim}}\lambda_{0}(i)

there are
i\in I
and
x_{1},\ldots,x_{n}\in\lambda_{0}(i)
such that
x_{l}
represents
\textnormal{{eql}}_{0}(z_{l})
, for
every
l\in\{1,\ldots,n\}
.

Proof.

The proof is by induction on
{\mathbb{N}}^{+}
. We present only the case
n:=2
. Let
z:=(j,y),z{{}^{\prime}}:=(j{{}^{\prime}},y{{}^{\prime}})\in\sum_{i\in I}^{\preccurlyeq}\lambda_{0}(i)
, and
k\in I
with
j\preccurlyeq k
and
j{{}^{\prime}}\preccurlyeq k
. By definition we have that
\lambda_{jk}^{\preccurlyeq}(y)\in\lambda_{0}(k)
and

\lambda_{j{{}^{\prime}}k}^{\preccurlyeq}(y{{}^{\prime}})\in\lambda_{0}(k)
. We show that
\lambda_{jk}^{\preccurlyeq}(y)
represents
\textnormal{{eql}}_{0}(z)

and
\lambda_{j{{}^{\prime}}k}^{\preccurlyeq}(y{{}^{\prime}})
represents
\textnormal{{eql}}_{0}(z{{}^{\prime}})
. By our remark right before
Definition
5.5.6
for the first representation
we need to show that

\omega_{k}\big(\lambda_{jk}^{\preccurlyeq}(y)\big)=_{\underset{\to}{\mathrm{Lim}}\lambda_{0}(i)}\omega_{j}(y)\Leftrightarrow\exists_{k{{}^{\prime}}\in I}\big(k\preccurlyeq k{{}^{\prime}}\ \&\ j\preccurlyeq k{{}^{\prime}}\ \&\ \lambda_{kk{{}^{\prime}}}^{\preccurlyeq}(\lambda_{jk}^{\preccurlyeq}(y))=_{\lambda_{0}(k{{}^{\prime}})}\lambda_{jk{{}^{\prime}}}^{\preccurlyeq}(y)\big).

By the composition of the transport maps it suffices to take any
k{{}^{\prime}}\in I
with
k\preccurlyeq k{{}^{\prime}}\ \&\ j\preccurlyeq k{{}^{\prime}}
,
and for the second representation it suffices to take any
k{{}^{\prime\prime}}\in I
with

k\preccurlyeq k{{}^{\prime\prime}}\ \&\ j{{}^{\prime}}\preccurlyeq k{{}^{\prime\prime}}
.
∎

Theorem 5.5.8
.

Let
S(\Lambda^{\preccurlyeq}):=(\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1}^{\preccurlyeq},\phi_{0}^{\Lambda^{\preccurlyeq}},\phi_{1}^{\Lambda^{\preccurlyeq}})\in\textnormal{{Spec}}(I,\preccurlyeq_{I})
with Bishop spaces
(F_{i})_{i\in I}
and Bishop morphisms
(\lambda_{ij}^{\preccurlyeq})_{(i,j)\in D^{\preccurlyeq}(I)}
,

S(M^{\preccurlyeq}):=(\mu_{0},\mu_{1}^{\preccurlyeq},\phi_{0}^{M^{\preccurlyeq}},\phi_{1}^{M^{\preccurlyeq}})\in\textnormal{{Spec}}(I,\preccurlyeq_{I})

with Bishop spaces
(G_{i})_{i\in I}

and Bishop morphisms
(\mu_{ij}^{\preccurlyeq})_{(i,j)\in D^{\preccurlyeq}(I)}
, and
\Psi\colon S(\Lambda^{\preccurlyeq})\Rightarrow S(M^{\preccurlyeq})
.

(i)

There is a unique function
\Psi_{\to}\colon\underset{\to}{\mathrm{Lim}}\lambda_{0}(i)\to\underset{\to}{\mathrm{Lim}}\mu_{0}(i)
such that, for every
i\in I
, the following diagram commutes
\underset{\to}{\mathrm{Lim}}\lambda_{0}(i)
\underset{\to}{\mathrm{Lim}}\mu_{0}(i)
.
\mu_{0}(i)
\lambda_{0}(i)
\Psi_{\to}
\Psi_{i}
\textnormal{{eql}}_{i}^{\Lambda^{\preccurlyeq}}
\textnormal{{eql}}_{i}^{M^{\preccurlyeq}}

(ii)

If
\Psi
is continuous, then

\Psi_{\to}\in\mathrm{Mor}(\underset{\to}{\mathrm{Lim}}\mathcal{F}_{i},\underset{\to}{\mathrm{Lim}}\mathcal{G}_{i})
.

(iii)

If
\Psi_{i}
is an embedding, for every
i\in I
,
then
\Psi_{\to}
is an embedding.

Proof.

(i) The following well-defined operation
\Psi_{\to}\colon\underset{\to}{\mathrm{Lim}}\lambda_{0}(i)\rightsquigarrow\underset{\to}{\mathrm{Lim}}\mu_{0}(i)
, given by

\Psi_{\to}\big(\textnormal{{eql}}_{0}^{\Lambda^{\preccurlyeq}}(i,x)\big):=\textnormal{{eql}}_{0}^{M^{\preccurlyeq}}(i,\Psi_{i}(x));\ \ \ \ \textnormal{{eql}}_{0}^{\Lambda^{\preccurlyeq}}(i,x)\in\underset{\to}{\mathrm{Lim}}\lambda_{0}(i)

is a function,
since, if

\textnormal{{eql}}_{0}^{\Lambda^{\preccurlyeq}}(i,x)=_{{\underset{\to}{\mathrm{Lim}}\lambda_{0}(i)}}\textnormal{{eql}}_{0}^{\Lambda^{\preccurlyeq}}(j,y)\Leftrightarrow(i,x)=_{{\sum_{i\in I}^{\prec}\lambda_{0}(i)}}(j,y)
, which is equivalent to

\exists_{k\in I}\big(i\preccurlyeq k\ \&\ j\preccurlyeq k\ \&\ \lambda_{ik}^{\preccurlyeq}(x)=_{{\lambda_{0}(k)}}\lambda_{jk}^{\preccurlyeq}(y)\big)
,
we show that

\displaystyle\Psi_{\to}\big(\textnormal{{eql}}_{0}^{\Lambda^{\preccurlyeq}}(i,x)\big)=_{{\underset{\to}{\mathrm{Lim}}\mu_{0}(i)}}\Psi_{\to}\big(\textnormal{{eql}}_{0}^{\Lambda^{\preccurlyeq}}(j,y)\big)

\displaystyle:\Leftrightarrow\textnormal{{eql}}_{0}^{M^{\preccurlyeq}}\big(i,\Psi_{i}^{\preccurlyeq}(x)\big)=_{{\underset{\to}{\mathrm{Lim}}\mu_{0}(i)}}\textnormal{{eql}}_{0}^{M^{\preccurlyeq}}\big(j,\Psi_{j}(y)\big)

\displaystyle\Leftrightarrow(i,\Psi_{i}(x))=_{{\sum_{i\in I}^{\preccurlyeq}\mu_{0}(i)}}(j,\Psi_{j}(y))

\displaystyle:\Leftrightarrow\exists_{i\in I}\big(i,j\preccurlyeq k\ \&\ \mu_{ik}^{\preccurlyeq}(\Psi_{i}(x))=_{{\mu_{0}(k)}}\mu_{jk}^{\preccurlyeq}(\Psi_{j}(y)\big).

By the commutativity of the following diagrams, and since
\Psi_{k}
is a function,
\mu_{0}(i)
\mu_{0}(k)
\lambda_{0}(k)
\lambda_{0}(i)
\mu_{0}(j)
\lambda_{0}(j)
\mu_{0}(k)
,
\lambda_{0}(k)
\mu_{ik}^{\preccurlyeq}
\lambda_{ik}^{\preccurlyeq}
\Psi_{i}
\Psi_{k}
\mu_{jk}^{\preccurlyeq}
\lambda_{jk}^{\preccurlyeq}
\Psi_{j}
\Psi_{k}

we get

\mu_{ik}^{\preccurlyeq}\big(\Psi_{i}(x)\big)=_{{\mu_{0}(k)}}\ \Psi_{k}\big(\lambda_{ik}^{\preccurlyeq}(x)\big)=_{{\mu_{0}(k)}}\ \Psi_{k}\big(\lambda_{jk}^{\preccurlyeq}(y)\big)=_{{\mu_{0}(k)}}\ \mu_{jk}^{\preccurlyeq}\big(\Psi_{j}(y)\big)
.

(ii) By the
\bigvee
-lifting of morphisms it suffices to show that

\forall_{H\in\prod_{i\in I}^{\succcurlyeq}G_{i}}\big((\textnormal{{eql}}_{0}^{M^{\preccurlyeq}}g_{H})\circ\Psi_{\to}\in\underset{\to}{\mathrm{Lim}}F_{i}\big).

By Definition
5.5.3
we have that

\big((\textnormal{{eql}}_{0}^{M^{\preccurlyeq}}g_{H})\circ\Psi_{\to}\big)\big(\textnormal{{eql}}_{0}^{\Lambda^{\preccurlyeq}}(i,x)\big):=\big(\textnormal{{eql}}_{0}^{\Lambda^{\preccurlyeq}}g_{H}\big)\big(\textnormal{{eql}}_{0}^{M^{\preccurlyeq}}(i,\Psi_{i}(x))\big):=g_{H}(i,\Psi_{i}(x))

:=H_{i}(\Psi_{i}(x))=(H_{i}\circ\Psi_{i})(x):=H_{i}^{*}(x):=f_{H^{*}}(i,x):=\big(\textnormal{{eql}}_{0}^{\Lambda^{\preccurlyeq}}f_{H^{*}}\big)\big(\textnormal{{eql}}_{0}^{\Lambda^{\preccurlyeq}}(i,x)\big),

where
H^{*}\in\prod_{i\in I}^{\succcurlyeq}F_{i}
is defined in Lemma
5.4.3
, and

(\textnormal{{eql}}_{0}^{M^{\preccurlyeq}}g_{H^{*}})\circ\Psi_{\to}:=\textnormal{{eql}}_{0}^{\Lambda^{\preccurlyeq}}f_{H^{*}}\in\underset{\to}{\mathrm{Lim}}F_{i}
.

(iii) If
\Psi_{\to}\big(\textnormal{{eql}}_{0}^{\Lambda^{\preccurlyeq}}(i,x)=_{{\underset{\to}{\mathrm{Lim}}\mu_{0}(i)}}\Psi_{\to}\big(\textnormal{{eql}}_{0}^{\Lambda^{\preccurlyeq}}(j,y)
i.e.,

\mu_{ik}^{\preccurlyeq}(\Psi_{i}(x))=_{{\mu_{0}(k)}}\mu_{jk}^{\preccurlyeq}(\Psi_{j}(y))\big)
,
for some
k\in I
with
i,j\preccurlyeq k
, by the proof of case (ii) we get

\Psi_{k}\big(\lambda_{ik}^{\preccurlyeq}(x)\big)=_{{\mu_{0}(k)}}\Psi_{k}\big(\lambda_{jk}^{\preccurlyeq}(y)\big)
, and since

\Psi_{k}
is an embedding, we conclude that
\lambda_{ik}^{\preccurlyeq}(x)=_{{\lambda_{0}(k)}}\lambda_{jk}^{\preccurlyeq}(y)
i.e.,
(i,x)=_{{\sum_{i\in I}^{\prec}\lambda_{0}(i)}}(j,y)
.
∎

Proposition 5.5.9
.

Let
S(\Lambda^{\preccurlyeq}):=(\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1}^{\preccurlyeq},\phi_{0}^{\Lambda^{\preccurlyeq}},\phi_{1}^{\Lambda^{\preccurlyeq}})\in\textnormal{{Spec}}(I,\preccurlyeq_{I})
with Bishop spaces
(F_{i})_{i\in I}
and Bishop morphisms
(\lambda_{ij}^{\preccurlyeq})_{(i,j)\in D^{\preccurlyeq}(I)}
,

S(M^{\preccurlyeq}):=(\mu_{0},\mu_{1}^{\preccurlyeq},\phi_{0}^{M^{\preccurlyeq}},\phi_{1}^{M^{\preccurlyeq}})\in\textnormal{{Spec}}(I,\preccurlyeq_{I})
with
Bishop spaces
(G_{i})_{i\in I}

and Bishop morphisms
(\mu_{ij}^{\preccurlyeq})_{(i,j)\in D^{\preccurlyeq}(I)}
, and

S(N^{\preccurlyeq}):=(\nu_{0},\nu_{1}^{\preccurlyeq},\phi_{0}^{N^{\preccurlyeq}},\phi_{1}^{N^{\preccurlyeq}})\in\textnormal{{Spec}}(I,\preccurlyeq_{I})
with
Bishop spaces
(H_{i})_{i\in I}

and Bishop morphisms
(\nu_{ij}^{\preccurlyeq})_{(i,j)\in D^{\preccurlyeq}(I)}
. If

\Psi\colon S(\Lambda^{\preccurlyeq})\Rightarrow S(M^{\preccurlyeq})
and
\Xi\colon S(M^{\preccurlyeq})\Rightarrow S(N^{\preccurlyeq})
, then

(\Xi\circ\Psi)_{\to}:=\Xi_{\to}\circ\Psi_{\to}
\lambda_{0}(i)
\mu_{0}(i)
\underset{\to}{\mathrm{Lim}}\lambda_{0}(i)
\underset{\to}{\mathrm{Lim}}\mu_{0}(i)
\nu_{0}(i)
\underset{\to}{\mathrm{Lim}}\nu_{0}(i)
.
\Psi_{i}
\Xi_{i}
\Xi_{\to}\ \
\ \ \Psi_{\to}
\textnormal{{eql}}_{i}^{\Lambda^{\preccurlyeq}}
\textnormal{{eql}}_{i}^{M^{\preccurlyeq}}
\textnormal{{eql}}_{i}^{N^{\preccurlyeq}}
(\Xi\circ\Psi)_{\to}
(\Xi\circ\Psi)_{i}

Proof.

If
\textnormal{{eql}}_{0}^{\Lambda^{\preccurlyeq}}(i,x)\in\underset{\to}{\mathrm{Lim}}\lambda_{0}(i)
, then

\displaystyle(\Xi\circ\Psi)_{\to}[\textnormal{{eql}}_{0}^{\Lambda^{\preccurlyeq}}(i,x)]

\displaystyle:=\textnormal{{eql}}_{0}^{N^{\preccurlyeq}}(i,(\Xi\circ\Psi)_{i}(x))

\displaystyle:=\textnormal{{eql}}_{0}^{N^{\preccurlyeq}}(i,(\Xi_{i}\circ\Psi_{i})(x))

\displaystyle:=\textnormal{{eql}}_{0}^{N^{\preccurlyeq}}(i,(\Xi_{i}(\Psi_{i}(x))))

\displaystyle:=\Xi_{\to}\big(\textnormal{{eql}}_{0}^{M^{\preccurlyeq}}(i,\Psi_{i}(x))\big)

\displaystyle:=\Xi_{\to}\big(\Psi_{\to}\big(\textnormal{{eql}}_{0}^{\Lambda^{\preccurlyeq}}(i,x)\big)\big)

\displaystyle:=(\Xi_{\to}\circ\Psi_{\to})\big(\textnormal{{eql}}_{0}^{\Lambda^{\preccurlyeq}}(i,x)\big).\qed

Definition 5.5.10
.

Let
S(\Lambda^{\preccurlyeq}):=(\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1}^{\preccurlyeq},\phi_{0}^{\Lambda^{\preccurlyeq}},\phi_{1}^{\Lambda^{\preccurlyeq}})\in\textnormal{{Spec}}(I,\preccurlyeq_{I})

and
(J,e,\textnormal{{cof}}_{J})\subseteq^{\textnormal{{cof}}}I
, a cofinal subset of
I
with modulus of cofinality
e\colon J\hookrightarrow I
.
The relative spectrum of

S(\Lambda^{\preccurlyeq})
to
J
is the
e
-subfamily

S(\Lambda^{\preccurlyeq})\circ e:=\big(\lambda_{0}\circ e,\lambda_{1}\circ e,\phi_{0}^{\Lambda^{\preccurlyeq}}\circ e,\phi_{1}^{\Lambda^{\preccurlyeq}}\circ e\big)
of
S(\Lambda^{\preccurlyeq})
, where
\Phi^{\Lambda^{\preccurlyeq}}\circ e:=\big(\phi_{0}^{\Lambda^{\preccurlyeq}}\circ e,\phi_{1}^{\Lambda^{\preccurlyeq}}\circ e\big)
is the
e
-subfamily
of
\Phi^{\Lambda^{\preccurlyeq}}
.

Lemma 5.5.11
.

Let
S(\Lambda^{\preccurlyeq}):=(\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1}^{\preccurlyeq},\phi_{0}^{\Lambda^{\preccurlyeq}},\phi_{1}^{\Lambda^{\preccurlyeq}})\in\textnormal{{Spec}}(I,\preccurlyeq_{I})
,

(J,e,\textnormal{{cof}}_{J})\subseteq^{\textnormal{{cof}}}I
, and
S(\Lambda^{\preccurlyeq})\circ e:=\big(\lambda_{0}\circ e,\lambda_{1}\circ e,\phi_{0}^{\Lambda^{\preccurlyeq}}\circ e,\phi_{1}^{\Lambda^{\preccurlyeq}}\circ e\big)
the relative spectrum of

S(\Lambda^{\preccurlyeq})
to
J
.

(i)

If
\Theta\in\prod_{i\in I}^{\succcurlyeq}F_{i}
, then
\Theta^{J}\in\prod_{j\in J}^{\succcurlyeq}F_{j}
, where
for every
j\in J
we define
\Theta^{J}_{j}:=\Theta_{e(j)}.

(ii)

If
H^{J}\in\prod_{j\in J}^{\succcurlyeq}F_{j}
, then
H\in\prod_{i\in I}^{\succcurlyeq}F_{i}
,
where, for every
i\in I
, let
H_{i}:=H^{J}_{\textnormal{{cof}}_{J}(i)}\circ\lambda_{ie(\textnormal{{cof}}_{J}(i))}^{\preccurlyeq}
{\mathbb{R}}
.
\lambda_{0}(e(\textnormal{{cof}}_{J}(i)))
\lambda_{0}(i)
H^{J}_{\textnormal{{cof}}_{J}(i)}
H_{i}\ \ \
\lambda_{ie(\textnormal{{cof}}_{J}(i))}^{\preccurlyeq}

Proof.

(i) It suffices to show that if
j\preccurlyeq j{{}^{\prime}}:\Leftrightarrow e(j)\preccurlyeq e(j{{}^{\prime}})
, then
\Theta^{J}_{j}=\Theta^{J}_{j{{}^{\prime}}}\circ\lambda_{jj{{}^{\prime}}}^{\preccurlyeq}
. Since
\Theta\in\prod_{i\in I}^{\succcurlyeq}F_{i}
we have that

\Theta^{J}_{j}:=\Theta_{e(j)}=\Theta_{e(j{{}^{\prime}})}\circ\lambda_{e(j)e(j{{}^{\prime}})}^{\preccurlyeq}:=\Theta^{J}_{j{{}^{\prime}}}\circ\lambda_{jj{{}^{\prime}}}^{\preccurlyeq}
.

(ii) By definition
H^{J}_{\textnormal{{cof}}_{J}(i)}\in F_{\textnormal{{cof}}_{J}(i)}:=F_{e(\textnormal{{cof}}_{J}(i))}
, and since
i\preccurlyeq e(\textnormal{{cof}}_{J}(i))
,
we get
H_{i}\in\mathrm{Mor}(\mathcal{F}_{i},\mathcal{R})=\mathcal{F}_{i}
i.e.,
H:\bigcurlywedge_{i\in I}F_{i}
. Next we show that if

i\preccurlyeq i{{}^{\prime}}
, then
H_{i}=H_{i{{}^{\prime}}}\circ\lambda_{ii{{}^{\prime}}}^{\preccurlyeq}
. By
(\textnormal{{Cof}}_{3})
and
(\textnormal{{Cof}}_{2})
we have that

i\preccurlyeq i{{}^{\prime}}\preccurlyeq e(\textnormal{{cof}}_{J}(i{{}^{\prime}})),

(5.1)

and
i\preccurlyeq i{{}^{\prime}}\Rightarrow\textnormal{{cof}}_{J}(i)\preccurlyeq\textnormal{{cof}}_{J}(i{{}^{\prime}}):\Leftrightarrow e(\textnormal{{cof}}_{J}(i))\preccurlyeq e(\textnormal{{cof}}_{J}(i{{}^{\prime}})),

hence we also get

i\preccurlyeq e(\textnormal{{cof}}_{J}(i))\preccurlyeq e(\textnormal{{cof}}_{J}(i{{}^{\prime}})).

(5.2)

Since
H^{J}\in\prod_{j\in J}^{\succcurlyeq}F_{j}
, we have that

\displaystyle H_{i{{}^{\prime}}}\circ\lambda_{ii{{}^{\prime}}}^{\preccurlyeq}

\displaystyle:=\big[H^{J}_{\textnormal{{cof}}_{J}(i{{}^{\prime}})}\circ\lambda_{i{{}^{\prime}}e(\textnormal{{cof}}_{J}(i{{}^{\prime}}))}^{\preccurlyeq}\big]\circ\lambda_{ii{{}^{\prime}}}^{\preccurlyeq}

\displaystyle:=H^{J}_{\textnormal{{cof}}_{J}(i{{}^{\prime}})}\circ\big[\lambda_{i{{}^{\prime}}e(\textnormal{{cof}}_{J}(i{{}^{\prime}}))}^{\preccurlyeq}\circ\lambda_{ii{{}^{\prime}}}^{\preccurlyeq}\big]

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(\ref{eq: eq1})}}{{=}}H^{J}_{\textnormal{{cof}}_{J}(i{{}^{\prime}})}\circ\lambda_{ie(\textnormal{{cof}}_{J}(i{{}^{\prime}}))}^{\preccurlyeq}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(\ref{eq: eq2})}}{{=}}H^{J}_{\textnormal{{cof}}_{J}(i{{}^{\prime}})}\circ\big[\lambda_{e(\textnormal{{cof}}_{J}(i))e(\textnormal{{cof}}_{J}(i{{}^{\prime}}))}^{\preccurlyeq}\circ\lambda_{ie(\textnormal{{cof}}_{J}(i))}^{\preccurlyeq}\big]

\displaystyle:=\big[H^{J}_{\textnormal{{cof}}_{J}(i{{}^{\prime}})}\circ\lambda_{e(\textnormal{{cof}}_{J}(i))e(\textnormal{{cof}}_{J}(i{{}^{\prime}}))}^{\preccurlyeq}\big]\circ\lambda_{ie(\textnormal{{cof}}_{J}(i))}^{\preccurlyeq}

\displaystyle:=\big[H^{J}_{\textnormal{{cof}}_{J}(i{{}^{\prime}})}\circ\lambda_{\textnormal{{cof}}_{J}(i)\textnormal{{cof}}_{J}(i{{}^{\prime}})}^{\preccurlyeq}\big]\circ\lambda_{ie(\textnormal{{cof}}_{J}(i))}^{\preccurlyeq}

\displaystyle:=H^{J}_{\textnormal{{cof}}_{J}(i)}\circ\lambda_{ie(\textnormal{{cof}}_{J}(i))}^{\preccurlyeq}

\displaystyle:=H_{i}.\qed

Theorem 5.5.12
.

Let
S(\Lambda^{\preccurlyeq}):=(\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1}^{\preccurlyeq},\phi_{0}^{\Lambda^{\preccurlyeq}},\phi_{1}^{\Lambda^{\preccurlyeq}})\in\textnormal{{Spec}}(I,\preccurlyeq_{I})
,

(J,e,\textnormal{{cof}}_{J})\subseteq^{\textnormal{{cof}}}I
, and
S(\Lambda^{\preccurlyeq})\circ e:=\big(\lambda_{0}\circ e,\lambda_{1}\circ e,\phi_{0}^{\Lambda^{\preccurlyeq}}\circ e,\phi_{1}^{\Lambda^{\preccurlyeq}}\circ e\big)
the relative spectrum of

S(\Lambda^{\preccurlyeq})
to
J
.
Then

\underset{\to}{\mathrm{Lim}}\mathcal{F}_{j}\simeq\underset{\to}{\mathrm{Lim}}\mathcal{F}_{i}.

Proof.

We define the operation
\phi:\underset{\to}{\mathrm{Lim}}\lambda_{0}(j)\rightsquigarrow\underset{\to}{\mathrm{Lim}}\lambda_{0}(i)
by

\phi\big(\textnormal{{eql}}_{0}^{\Lambda^{\preccurlyeq}\circ e}(j,y)\big):=\textnormal{{eql}}_{0}^{\Lambda^{\preccurlyeq}}(e(j),y)
\underset{\to}{\mathrm{Lim}}\lambda_{0}(j)
\underset{\to}{\mathrm{Lim}}\lambda_{0}(i)
,
\lambda_{0}(j)
\phi
\textnormal{{eql}}_{j}^{\Lambda^{\preccurlyeq}\circ e}\
\ \textnormal{{eql}}_{e(j)}^{\Lambda^{\preccurlyeq}}

for every
\textnormal{{eql}}_{0}^{\Lambda^{\preccurlyeq}\circ e}(j,y)\in\underset{\to}{\mathrm{Lim}}\lambda_{0}(j)
,
where, if
j\in J
and
y\in\lambda_{0}(j)
, we have that

\textnormal{{eql}}_{0}^{\Lambda^{\preccurlyeq}\circ e}(j,y):=\bigg\{(j{{}^{\prime}},y{{}^{\prime}})\in\sum_{j\in J}^{\preccurlyeq}\lambda_{0}(j)\mid(j{{}^{\prime}},y{{}^{\prime}})=_{{\sum_{j\in J}^{\preccurlyeq}\lambda_{0}(j)}}(j,y)\bigg\},

\textnormal{{eql}}_{0}^{\Lambda^{\preccurlyeq}}(e(j),y):=\bigg\{(i,x)\in\sum_{i\in I}^{\preccurlyeq}\lambda_{0}(i)\mid(i,x)=_{{\sum_{i\in I}^{\preccurlyeq}\lambda_{0}(i)}}(e(j),y)\bigg\}.

First we show that
\phi
is a function.
By definition we have that

\displaystyle\textnormal{{eql}}_{0}^{\Lambda^{\preccurlyeq}\circ e}(j,y)=_{{\underset{\to}{\mathrm{Lim}}\lambda_{0}(j)}}\textnormal{{eql}}_{0}^{\Lambda^{\preccurlyeq}\circ e}(j{{}^{\prime}},y{{}^{\prime}})

\displaystyle\Leftrightarrow(j,y)=_{{\sum_{j\in J}^{\preccurlyeq}\lambda_{0}(j)}}(j{{}^{\prime}},y{{}^{\prime}})

\displaystyle\Leftrightarrow\exists_{j{{}^{\prime\prime}}\in J}\big(j,j{{}^{\prime}}\preccurlyeq j{{}^{\prime\prime}}\ \&\ \lambda_{jj{{}^{\prime\prime}}}^{\preccurlyeq}(y)=_{{\lambda_{0}(j{{}^{\prime\prime}})}}\lambda_{j{{}^{\prime}}j{{}^{\prime\prime}}}^{\preccurlyeq}(y{{}^{\prime}})\big)\ \ \ (1)

\displaystyle\textnormal{{eql}}_{0}^{\Lambda^{\preccurlyeq}}(e(j),y)=_{{\underset{\to}{\mathrm{Lim}}\lambda_{0}(i)}}\textnormal{{eql}}_{0}^{\Lambda^{\preccurlyeq}}(e(j{{}^{\prime}}),y{{}^{\prime}})

\displaystyle\Leftrightarrow(e(j),y)=_{{\sum_{i\in I}^{\preccurlyeq}\lambda_{0}(i)}}(e(j{{}^{\prime}}),y{{}^{\prime}})

\displaystyle\Leftrightarrow\exists_{k\in I}\big(e(j),e(j{{}^{\prime}})\preccurlyeq k\ \&\ \lambda_{e(j)k}^{\preccurlyeq}(y)=_{{\lambda_{0}(k)}}\lambda_{e(j{{}^{\prime}})k}^{\preccurlyeq}(y{{}^{\prime}})\big).\ (2)

If
k:=e(j{{}^{\prime\prime}})
, then (1) implies (2), and hence
\phi
is a function. To show that
\phi
is an embedding,
we show that (2) implies (1). Since
e(j),e(j{{}^{\prime}})\preccurlyeq k\preccurlyeq e(\textnormal{{cof}}_{J}(k))
,
we get
j,j{{}^{\prime}}\preccurlyeq\textnormal{{cof}}_{J}(k):=j{{}^{\prime\prime}}
.
By the commutativity of the following diagrams
\lambda_{0}(e(j))
\lambda_{0}(k)
\lambda_{0}(e(\textnormal{{cof}}_{J}(k)))
\lambda_{0}(k)
\lambda_{0}(e(j{{}^{\prime}}))
\lambda_{0}(e(\textnormal{{cof}}_{J}(k)))
\lambda_{e(j)k}^{\preccurlyeq}
\ \ \ \lambda_{e(j)e(\textnormal{{cof}}_{J}(k))}^{\preccurlyeq}
\lambda_{ke(\textnormal{{cof}}_{J}(k))}^{\preccurlyeq}
\lambda_{e(j{{}^{\prime}})k}^{\preccurlyeq}
\lambda_{ke(\textnormal{{cof}}_{J}(k))}^{\preccurlyeq}
\ \ \ \lambda_{e(j{{}^{\prime}})e(\textnormal{{cof}}_{J}(k))}^{\preccurlyeq}

\displaystyle\lambda_{jj{{}^{\prime\prime}}}^{\preccurlyeq}(y)

\displaystyle:=\lambda_{e(j)e(\textnormal{{cof}}_{J}(k))}^{\preccurlyeq}(y)

\displaystyle=\big[\lambda_{ke(\textnormal{{cof}}_{J}(k))}\circ\lambda_{e(j)k}^{\preccurlyeq}\big](y)

\displaystyle=\lambda_{ke(\textnormal{{cof}}_{J}(k))}\big(\lambda_{e(j)k}^{\preccurlyeq}(y)\big)

\displaystyle=\lambda_{ke(\textnormal{{cof}}_{J}(k))}\big(\lambda_{e(j{{}^{\prime}})k}^{\preccurlyeq}(y{{}^{\prime}})\big)

\displaystyle:=\big[\lambda_{ke(\textnormal{{cof}}_{J}(k))}\circ\lambda_{e(j{{}^{\prime}})k}^{\preccurlyeq}\big](y{{}^{\prime}})

\displaystyle=\lambda_{e(j{{}^{\prime}})e(\textnormal{{cof}}_{J}(k))}^{\preccurlyeq}(y{{}^{\prime}})

\displaystyle:=\lambda_{j{{}^{\prime}}j{{}^{\prime\prime}}}(y{{}^{\prime}}).

By the
\bigvee
-lifting of morphisms we have that

\phi\in\mathrm{Mor}(\underset{\to}{\mathrm{Lim}}\mathcal{F}_{j},\underset{\to}{\mathrm{Lim}}\mathcal{F}_{i}):\Leftrightarrow\forall_{{\Theta\in\prod_{i\in I}^{\succcurlyeq}F_{i}}}\big(\textnormal{{eql}}_{0}f_{\Theta}\circ\phi\in\underset{\to}{\mathrm{Lim}}F_{j}\big).

If
\Theta\in\prod_{i\in I}^{\succcurlyeq}F_{i}
, we have that

(\textnormal{{eql}}_{0}f_{\Theta}\circ\phi)\big(\textnormal{{eql}}_{0}^{\Lambda^{\preccurlyeq}\circ e}(j,y)\big):=(\textnormal{{eql}}_{0}f_{\Theta})\big(\textnormal{{eql}}_{0}^{\Lambda^{\preccurlyeq}}(e(j),y)\big)

:=\Theta_{e(j)}(y):=\Theta^{J}_{j}(y):=(\textnormal{{eql}}_{0}f_{\Theta^{J}})\big(\textnormal{{eql}}_{0}^{\Lambda^{\preccurlyeq}\circ e}(j,y)\big),

where
\Theta^{J}\in\prod_{j\in J}^{\preccurlyeq}F_{j}
is defined in Lemma
5.5.11
(i).
Hence,
\textnormal{{eql}}_{0}f_{\Theta}\circ\phi=\textnormal{{eql}}_{0}f_{\Theta^{J}}\in\underset{\to}{\mathrm{Lim}}F_{j}
.
Next we show that
\phi
is a surjection. If
\textnormal{{eql}}_{0}^{\Lambda^{\preccurlyeq}}(i,x)\in\underset{\to}{\mathrm{Lim}}\lambda_{0}(i)
, we find
\textnormal{{eql}}_{0}^{\Lambda^{\preccurlyeq}\circ e}(j,y)\in\underset{\to}{\mathrm{Lim}}\lambda_{0}(j)
such that

\phi\big(\textnormal{{eql}}_{0}^{\Lambda^{\preccurlyeq}\circ e}(j,y)\big):=\textnormal{{eql}}_{0}^{\Lambda^{\preccurlyeq}}(e(j),y)=_{{\underset{\to}{\mathrm{Lim}}\lambda_{0}(i)}}\textnormal{{eql}}_{0}^{\Lambda^{\preccurlyeq}}(i,x)
i.e.,
we find
k\in I
such that

i,e(j)\preccurlyeq k
and
\lambda_{ik}^{\preccurlyeq}(x)=_{{\lambda_{0}(k)}}\lambda_{e(j)k}^{\preccurlyeq}(y)
.
If
j:=\textnormal{{cof}}_{J}(i)
, by
(\textnormal{{Cof}}_{3})
we have that
i\preccurlyeq e(\textnormal{{cof}}_{J}(i))
,
and by the reflexivity of
\preccurlyeq
we have that
e(\textnormal{{cof}}_{J}(i))\preccurlyeq e(\textnormal{{cof}}_{J}(i)):=k
. If

y:=\lambda_{ie(\textnormal{{cof}}_{J}(i))}{{}^{\preccurlyeq}}(x)\in\lambda_{0}(e(\textnormal{{cof}}_{J}(i))):=(\lambda_{0}\circ e)(\textnormal{{cof}}_{J}(i))
, then

\lambda_{e(\textnormal{{cof}}_{J}(i))e(\textnormal{{cof}}_{J}(i))}^{\preccurlyeq}\big(\lambda_{ie(\textnormal{{cof}}_{J}(i))}^{\preccurlyeq}(x)\big)=_{{\lambda_{0}(k)}}\lambda_{ie(\textnormal{{cof}}_{J}(i))}^{\preccurlyeq}(x).

We can use the
\bigvee
-lifting of openness to show that
\phi
is an open morphism, and hence a
Bishop isomorphism, but it is better to define directly its inverse Bishop morphism using the previous
proof of the surjectivity
of
\phi
. Let the operation
\theta\colon\underset{\to}{\mathrm{Lim}}\lambda_{0}(i)\rightsquigarrow\underset{\to}{\mathrm{Lim}}\lambda_{0}(j)
,
defined by

\theta\big(\textnormal{{eql}}_{0}^{\Lambda^{\preccurlyeq}}(i,x)\big):=\textnormal{{eql}}_{0}^{\Lambda^{\preccurlyeq}\circ e}\big(\textnormal{{cof}}_{J}(i),\lambda_{ie(\textnormal{{cof}}_{J}(i))}(x)\big);\ \ \ \ \textnormal{{eql}}_{0}^{\Lambda^{\preccurlyeq}}(i,x)\in\underset{\to}{\mathrm{Lim}}\lambda_{0}(i).

First we show that
\theta
is a function. We have that

\textnormal{{eql}}_{0}^{\Lambda^{\preccurlyeq}}(i,x)=_{{\underset{\to}{\mathrm{Lim}}\lambda_{0}(i)}}\textnormal{{eql}}_{0}^{\Lambda^{\preccurlyeq}}(i{{}^{\prime}},x{{}^{\prime}})\Leftrightarrow\exists_{k\in I}\big(i\preccurlyeq k\ \&\ i{{}^{\prime}}\preccurlyeq k\ \&\ \lambda_{ik}^{\preccurlyeq}(x)=_{{\lambda_{0}(k)}}\lambda_{i{{}^{\prime}}k}^{\preccurlyeq}(x{{}^{\prime}})\big),

\textnormal{{eql}}_{0}^{\Lambda^{\preccurlyeq}\circ e}\big(\textnormal{{cof}}_{J}(i),\lambda_{ie(\textnormal{{cof}}_{J}(i))}(x)\big)=_{{\underset{\to}{\mathrm{Lim}}\lambda_{0}(j)}}\textnormal{{eql}}_{0}^{\Lambda^{\preccurlyeq}\circ e}\big(\textnormal{{cof}}_{J}(i{{}^{\prime}}),\lambda_{i{{}^{\prime}}e(\textnormal{{cof}}_{J}(i{{}^{\prime}}))}(x{{}^{\prime}})\big)\Leftrightarrow

\exists_{j{{}^{\prime}}\in J}\bigg(\textnormal{{cof}}_{J}(i)\preccurlyeq j{{}^{\prime}}\ \&\ \textnormal{{cof}}_{J}(i{{}^{\prime}})\preccurlyeq j{{}^{\prime}}\ \&

\lambda^{\preccurlyeq}_{e(\textnormal{{cof}}_{J}(i))e(j{{}^{\prime}})}\big(\lambda^{\preccurlyeq}_{ie(\textnormal{{cof}}_{J}(i))}(x)\big)=_{{\lambda_{0}(e(j{{}^{\prime}})}}\lambda^{\preccurlyeq}_{e(\textnormal{{cof}}_{J}(i{{}^{\prime}}))e(j{{}^{\prime}})}\big(\lambda^{\preccurlyeq}_{i{{}^{\prime}}e(\textnormal{{cof}}_{J}(i{{}^{\prime}}))}(x{{}^{\prime}})\bigg).

If
j{{}^{\prime}}:=\textnormal{{cof}}_{J}(k)
, then by
(\textnormal{{Cof}}_{2})
we get
\textnormal{{cof}}_{J}(i)\preccurlyeq j{{}^{\prime}}
and
\textnormal{{cof}}_{J}(i{{}^{\prime}})\preccurlyeq j{{}^{\prime}}
.
Next we show that

\lambda^{\preccurlyeq}_{e(\textnormal{{cof}}_{J}(i))e(\textnormal{{cof}}_{J}(k))}\big(\lambda^{\preccurlyeq}_{ie(\textnormal{{cof}}_{J}(i))}(x)\big)=_{{\lambda_{0}(e(\textnormal{{cof}}_{J}(k))}}\lambda^{\preccurlyeq}_{e(\textnormal{{cof}}_{J}(i{{}^{\prime}}))e(\textnormal{{cof}}_{J}(k))}\big(\lambda^{\preccurlyeq}_{i{{}^{\prime}}e(\textnormal{{cof}}_{J}(i{{}^{\prime}}))}(x{{}^{\prime}}).

By the following order relations, the two terms of the required equality are written as
e(\textnormal{{cof}}_{J}(k))
k
e(\textnormal{{cof}}_{J}(i{{}^{\prime}}))
e(\textnormal{{cof}}_{J}(i))
i
i{{}^{\prime}}

\lambda^{\preccurlyeq}_{ie(\textnormal{{cof}}_{J}(k))}(x)=\lambda^{\preccurlyeq}_{ke(\textnormal{{cof}}_{J}(k))}\big(\lambda^{\preccurlyeq}_{ik}(x)\big)
,
and
\lambda^{\preccurlyeq}_{i{{}^{\prime}}e(\textnormal{{cof}}_{J}(k))}(x{{}^{\prime}})=\lambda^{\preccurlyeq}_{ke(\textnormal{{cof}}_{J}(k))}\big(\lambda^{\preccurlyeq}_{i{{}^{\prime}}k}(x{{}^{\prime}})\big)
.
By the equality

\lambda_{ik}^{\preccurlyeq}(x)=_{{\lambda_{0}(k)}}\lambda_{i{{}^{\prime}}k}^{\preccurlyeq}(x{{}^{\prime}})
we get the required equality.
Next we show that

\theta\in\mathrm{Mor}\big(\underset{\to}{\mathrm{Lim}}\mathcal{F}_{i},\underset{\to}{\mathrm{Lim}}\mathcal{F}_{j}\big)\Leftrightarrow\forall_{H^{J}\in\prod_{j\in J}^{\succcurlyeq}F_{j}}\bigg(\textnormal{{eql}}_{0}f_{H^{J}}\circ\theta\in\bigvee_{\Theta\in\prod_{i\in I}^{\succcurlyeq}F_{i}}\textnormal{{eql}}_{0}f_{\Theta}\bigg).

If we fix
H^{J}\in\prod_{j\in J}^{\succcurlyeq}F_{j}
, and if
H\in\prod_{i\in I}^{\succcurlyeq}F_{i}
, defined
in Lemma
5.5.11
(ii), then

\displaystyle\big(\textnormal{{eql}}_{0}f_{H^{J}}\circ\theta\big)\big(\textnormal{{eql}}_{0}^{\Lambda^{\preccurlyeq}}(i,x)\big)

\displaystyle:=\textnormal{{eql}}_{0}f_{H^{J}}\bigg(\textnormal{{eql}}_{0}^{\Lambda^{\preccurlyeq}\circ e}\big(\textnormal{{cof}}_{J}(i),\lambda_{ie(\textnormal{{cof}}_{J}(i))}(x)\big)\bigg)

\displaystyle:=f_{H^{J}}\big(\textnormal{{cof}}_{J}(i),\lambda_{ie(\textnormal{{cof}}_{J}(i))}(x)\big)

\displaystyle:=H^{J}_{\textnormal{{cof}}_{J}(i)}\big(\lambda_{ie(\textnormal{{cof}}_{J}(i))}(x)\big)

\displaystyle:=\big[H^{J}_{\textnormal{{cof}}_{J}(i)}\circ\lambda_{ie(\textnormal{{cof}}_{J}(i))}\big](x)

\displaystyle:=H_{i}(x)

\displaystyle:=f_{H}(i,x)

\displaystyle:=\textnormal{{eql}}_{0}f_{H}\big(\textnormal{{eql}}_{0}^{\Lambda^{\preccurlyeq}}(i,x)\big),

hence
\textnormal{{eql}}_{0}f_{H^{J}}\circ\theta:=\textnormal{{eql}}_{0}f_{H}\in\underset{\to}{\mathrm{Lim}}F_{i}
. Next we show that

\phi
and
\theta
are inverse to each other.

\displaystyle\phi\big(\theta\big(\textnormal{{eql}}_{0}^{\Lambda^{\preccurlyeq}}(i,x)\big)\big)

\displaystyle:=\phi\big(\textnormal{{eql}}_{0}^{\Lambda^{\preccurlyeq}\circ e}\big(\textnormal{{cof}}_{J}(i),\lambda_{ie(\textnormal{{cof}}_{J}(i))}(x)\big)

\displaystyle:=\textnormal{{eql}}_{0}^{\Lambda^{\preccurlyeq}}\big(e(\textnormal{{cof}}_{J}(i)),\lambda_{ie(\textnormal{{cof}}_{J}(i))}(x)\big),

which is equal to
\textnormal{{eql}}_{0}^{\Lambda^{\preccurlyeq}}(i,x)
if and only if there is
k\in I
with
i\preccurlyeq k
and

e(\textnormal{{cof}}_{J}(i))\preccurlyeq k
and

\lambda_{ik}^{\preccurlyeq}(x)=_{{\lambda_{0}(k)}}\lambda^{\preccurlyeq}_{e(\textnormal{{cof}}_{J}(i))k}\big(\lambda_{ie(\textnormal{{cof}}_{J}(i))}(x)\big),

which holds for every such
k\in I
. As by
(\textnormal{{Cof}}_{3})
we have that
i\preccurlyeq e(\textnormal{{cof}}_{J}(i))
, the existence
of such a
k\in I
follows trivially. Similarly,

\displaystyle\theta\big(\phi\big(\textnormal{{eql}}_{0}^{\Lambda^{\preccurlyeq}\circ e}(j,y)\big)\big)

\displaystyle:=\theta\big(\textnormal{{eql}}_{0}^{\Lambda^{\preccurlyeq}}\big(e(j),y)\big)

\displaystyle:=\textnormal{{eql}}_{0}^{\Lambda^{\preccurlyeq}\circ e}\big(\textnormal{{cof}}_{J}(e(j)),\lambda_{e(j)e(\textnormal{{cof}}_{J}(e(j)))}(y)\big),

which is equal to
\textnormal{{eql}}_{0}^{\Lambda^{\preccurlyeq}\circ e}(j,y)
if and only if there is
j{{}^{\prime}}\in J

with
j\preccurlyeq j{{}^{\prime}}
,
(\textnormal{{cof}}_{J}(e(j)))\preccurlyeq j{{}^{\prime}}
and

\lambda_{e(j)e(j{{}^{\prime}})}^{\preccurlyeq}(y)=_{{\lambda_{0}(e(j{{}^{\prime}}))}}\lambda^{\preccurlyeq}_{e(\textnormal{{cof}}_{J}(e(j)))e(j{{}^{\prime}})}\big(\lambda_{e(j)e(\textnormal{{cof}}_{J}(e(j)))}(y)\big),

which holds for every such
j{{}^{\prime}}\in J
. As by
(\textnormal{{Cof}}_{1})
we have that
j=_{J}\textnormal{{cof}}_{J}(e(j))
, the existence of such a

j{{}^{\prime}}\in J
follows trivially.
∎

For simplicity we use next the same symbol for different orderings.

Proposition 5.5.13
.

If
(I,\preccurlyeq),(J,\preccurlyeq)
are directed sets,
i\in I
and
j\in J
, let

(i,j)\preccurlyeq(i{{}^{\prime}},j{{}^{\prime}}):\Leftrightarrow i\preccurlyeq i{{}^{\prime}}\ \&\ j\preccurlyeq j{{}^{\prime}}.

If
(K,i_{K},\textnormal{{cof}}_{K})\subseteq^{\textnormal{{cof}}}I
and
(L,i_{L},\textnormal{{cof}}_{L})\subseteq^{\textnormal{{cof}}}J
, let

i_{K\times L}:K\times L\hookrightarrow I\times J
and
\textnormal{{cof}}_{K\times L}:I\times J\to K\times L
,
defined, for every
k\in K
and
l\in L
, by

i_{K\times L}(k,l):=\big(i_{K}(k),i_{L}(l)\big)\ \ \ \&\ \ \ \textnormal{{cof}}_{K\times L}(i,j):=\big(\textnormal{{cof}}_{K}(i),\textnormal{{cof}}_{L}(j)\big).

Let
\Lambda^{\preccurlyeq}:=(\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1}^{\preccurlyeq})\in\textnormal{{Fam}}(I,\preccurlyeq)

and
M^{\preccurlyeq}:=(\mu_{0},\mu_{1}^{\preccurlyeq})\in\textnormal{{Fam}}(J,\preccurlyeq)
an
(J,\preccurlyeq)
. Let also

S(\Lambda^{\preccurlyeq}):=(\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1}^{\preccurlyeq},\phi_{0}^{\Lambda^{\preccurlyeq}},\phi_{1}^{\Lambda^{\preccurlyeq}})\in\textnormal{{Spec}}(I,\preccurlyeq)

with Bishop spaces
(\mathcal{F}_{i})_{i\in I}
and Bishop morphisms
(\lambda_{ii{{}^{\prime}}})_{(i,i{{}^{\prime}})\in D^{\preccurlyeq}(I\times J)}
,
and
S(M^{\preccurlyeq}):=(\mu_{0},\mu_{1}^{\preccurlyeq},\phi_{0}^{M^{\preccurlyeq}},\phi_{1}^{M^{\preccurlyeq}})\in\textnormal{{Spec}}(J,\preccurlyeq)

with Bishop spaces
(\mathcal{G}_{j})_{j\in J}
and Bishop morphisms
(\mu_{jj{{}^{\prime}}})_{(j,j{{}^{\prime}})\in\preccurlyeq(J)}^{\preccurlyeq}
.

(i)

(I\times J,\preccurlyeq)
is a directed set, and
(K\times L,i_{K\times L},\textnormal{{cof}}_{K\times L})\subseteq^{\textnormal{{cof}}}I\times J
.

(ii)

The pair
\Lambda^{\preccurlyeq}\times M^{\preccurlyeq}:=(\lambda_{0}\times\mu_{0},(\lambda_{1}\times\mu_{1})^{\preccurlyeq})\in\textnormal{{Fam}}(I\times J,\preccurlyeq)
, where

(\lambda_{0}\times\mu_{0})\big((i,j)\big):=\lambda_{0}(i)\times\mu_{0}(j),

(\lambda_{1}\times\mu_{1})^{\preccurlyeq}\big((i,j),(i{{}^{\prime}},j{{}^{\prime}})\big):=(\lambda_{1}\times\mu_{1})^{\preccurlyeq}_{(i,j),(i{{}^{\prime}},j{{}^{\prime}})},

(\lambda_{1}\times\mu_{1})^{\preccurlyeq}_{(i,j),(i{{}^{\prime}},j{{}^{\prime}})}\big((x,y)\big):=\big(\lambda_{ii{{}^{\prime}}}^{\preccurlyeq}(x),\mu_{jj{{}^{\prime}}}^{\preccurlyeq}(y)\big).

(iii)

The structure

S(\Lambda^{\preccurlyeq}\times M^{\preccurlyeq}):=\big(\lambda_{0}\times\mu_{0},\lambda_{1}^{\preccurlyeq}\times\mu_{1}^{\preccurlyeq};\phi_{0}^{\Lambda^{\preccurlyeq}\times M^{\preccurlyeq}},\phi_{1}^{\Lambda^{\preccurlyeq}\times M^{\preccurlyeq}}\big)\in\textnormal{{Spec}}(I\times J,\preccurlyeq)

with Bishop spaces
(F_{i}\times G_{j})_{(i,j)\in I\times J}
and Bishop morphisms

(\lambda_{1}\times\mu_{1})_{\big((i,j)(i{{}^{\prime}},j{{}^{\prime}})\big)\in D^{\preccurlyeq}(I\times J)}
, where

\phi_{0}^{\Lambda^{\preccurlyeq}\times M^{\preccurlyeq}}(i,j):=F_{i}\times G_{j},

\phi_{1}^{\Lambda^{\preccurlyeq}\times M^{\preccurlyeq}}\big((i,j),(i{{}^{\prime}},j{{}^{\prime}})\big):=[(\lambda_{1}\times\mu_{1})_{(i,j)(i{{}^{\prime}},j{{}^{\prime}})}^{\preccurlyeq}]^{*}:F_{i{{}^{\prime}}}\times G_{j{{}^{\prime}}}\to F_{i}\times G_{j}.

Proof.

(i) is immediate to show. For the proof of case (ii) we have that

(\lambda_{1}\times\mu_{1})^{\preccurlyeq}_{(i,j),(i,j)}\big((x,y)\big):=\big(\lambda_{ii}^{\preccurlyeq}(x),\mu_{jj}^{\preccurlyeq}(y)\big):=(x,y),

and if
(i,j)\preccurlyeq(i{{}^{\prime}},j{{}^{\prime}})\preccurlyeq(i{{}^{\prime\prime}},j{{}^{\prime\prime}})
, then the commutativity of the
\lambda_{0}(i)\times\mu_{0}(j)
\lambda_{0}(i{{}^{\prime}})\times\mu_{0}(j{{}^{\prime}})
\lambda_{0}(i{{}^{\prime\prime}})\times\mu_{0}(j{{}^{\prime\prime}})
{(\lambda_{1}\times\mu_{1})^{\preccurlyeq}_{(i,j),(i{{}^{\prime}},j{{}^{\prime}})}}
\ \ \ {(\lambda_{1}\times\mu_{1})^{\preccurlyeq}_{(i,j),(i{{}^{\prime\prime}},j{{}^{\prime\prime}})}}
{(\lambda_{1}\times\mu_{1})^{\preccurlyeq}_{(i{{}^{\prime}},j{{}^{\prime}}),(i{{}^{\prime\prime}},j{{}^{\prime\prime}})}}

above diagram follows from the equalities
\lambda_{ii{{}^{\prime\prime}}}^{\preccurlyeq}=\lambda_{i{{}^{\prime}}i{{}^{\prime\prime}}}^{\preccurlyeq}\circ\lambda_{ii{{}^{\prime}}}^{\preccurlyeq}

and
\mu_{jj{{}^{\prime\prime}}}^{\preccurlyeq}=\mu_{j{{}^{\prime}}j{{}^{\prime\prime}}}^{\preccurlyeq}\circ\mu_{jj{{}^{\prime}}}^{\preccurlyeq}
.

(iii) We show that
(\lambda_{1}\times\mu_{1})_{(i,j)(i{{}^{\prime}},j{{}^{\prime}})}^{\preccurlyeq}\in\mathrm{Mor}(\mathcal{F}_{i}\times\mathcal{G}_{j},\mathcal{F}_{i{{}^{\prime}}}\times\mathcal{G}_{j{{}^{\prime}}})
. By the
\bigvee
-lifting of morphisms it suffices to show that

\forall_{f\in F_{i{{}^{\prime}}}}\big((f\circ\pi_{1})\circ(\lambda_{1}\times\mu_{1})_{(i,j)(i{{}^{\prime}},j{{}^{\prime}})}^{\preccurlyeq}\in F_{i}\times G_{j}\big)
and
\forall_{g\in G_{j{{}^{\prime}}}}\big((g\circ\pi_{2})\circ(\lambda_{1}\times\mu_{1})_{(i,j)(i{{}^{\prime}},j{{}^{\prime}})}^{\preccurlyeq}\in F_{i}\times G_{j}\big).

If
f\in F_{i{{}^{\prime}}}
, then
(f\circ\pi_{1})\circ(\lambda_{1}\times\mu_{1})_{(i,j)(i{{}^{\prime}},j{{}^{\prime}})}^{\preccurlyeq}:=(f\circ\lambda_{ii{{}^{\prime}}}^{\preccurlyeq})\circ\pi_{1}\in F_{i}\times G_{j}
, as
f\circ\lambda_{ii{{}^{\prime}}}^{\preccurlyeq}\in F_{i}
and

[(f\circ\pi_{1})\circ(\lambda_{1}\times\mu_{1})_{(i,j)(i{{}^{\prime}},j{{}^{\prime}})}^{\preccurlyeq}](x,y):=(f\circ\pi_{1})\big(\lambda_{ii{{}^{\prime}}}^{\preccurlyeq}(x),\mu_{jj{{}^{\prime}}}^{\preccurlyeq}(y)\big):=f\big(\lambda_{ii{{}^{\prime}}}^{\preccurlyeq}(x)\big):=[(f\circ\lambda_{ii{{}^{\prime}}}^{\preccurlyeq})\circ\pi_{1}](x,y)
.
If
g\in G_{j{{}^{\prime}}}
, we get
(g\circ\pi_{2})\circ(\lambda_{1}\times\mu_{1})_{(i,j)(i{{}^{\prime}},j{{}^{\prime}})}^{\preccurlyeq}:=(g\circ\lambda_{jj{{}^{\prime}}}^{\preccurlyeq})\circ\pi_{2}\in F_{i}\times G_{j}
.
∎

Lemma 5.5.14
.

Let
S(\Lambda^{\preccurlyeq}):=(\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1}^{\preccurlyeq},\phi_{0}^{\Lambda^{\preccurlyeq}},\phi_{1}^{\Lambda^{\preccurlyeq}})\in\textnormal{{Spec}}(I,\preccurlyeq)

with Bishop spaces
(\mathcal{F}_{i})_{i\in I}
and Bishop morphisms

(\lambda_{ii{{}^{\prime}}})_{(i,i{{}^{\prime}})\in D^{\preccurlyeq}(I)}^{\preccurlyeq}
,

S(M^{\preccurlyeq}):=(\mu_{0},\mu_{1}^{\preccurlyeq},\phi_{0}^{M^{\preccurlyeq}},\phi_{1}^{M^{\preccurlyeq}})\in\textnormal{{Spec}}(J,\preccurlyeq)

with Bishop spaces
(\mathcal{G}_{j})_{j\in J}
and Bishop morphisms
(\mu_{jj{{}^{\prime}}})_{(j,j{{}^{\prime}})\in D^{\preccurlyeq}(J)}
,

\Theta\in\prod_{i\in I}^{\succcurlyeq}F_{i}
and
\Phi\in\prod_{j\in J}^{\succcurlyeq}G_{j}
,. Then

\Theta_{1}\in\prod_{(i,j)\in I\times J}^{\succcurlyeq}F_{i}\times G_{j}\ \ \ \&\ \ \ \ \Phi_{2}\in\prod_{(i,j)\in I\times J}^{\succcurlyeq}F_{i}\times G_{j},

\Theta_{1}(i,j):=\Theta_{i}\circ\pi_{1}\in F_{i}\times G_{j}\ \ \ \&\ \ \ \Phi_{2}(i,j):=\Phi_{j}\circ\pi_{2}\in F_{i}\times G_{j};\ \ \ \ (i,j)\in I\times J.

Proof.

We prove that
\Theta_{1}\in\prod_{(i,j)\in I\times J}^{\succcurlyeq}F_{i}\times G_{j}
, and for

\Phi_{2}
we proceed similarly. If
(i,j)\preccurlyeq(i{{}^{\prime}},j{{}^{\prime}})
, we need to show that

\Theta_{1}(i,j)=\Theta_{1}(i{{}^{\prime}},j{{}^{\prime}})\circ(\lambda_{1}\times\mu_{1})^{\preccurlyeq}_{(i,j),(i{{}^{\prime}},j{{}^{\prime}})}
.
Since
\Theta\in\prod_{i\in I}^{\succcurlyeq}F_{i}
, we have that

\Theta_{i}=\Theta_{i{{}^{\prime}}}\circ\lambda_{ii{{}^{\prime}}}^{\preccurlyeq}
.
If
x\in\lambda_{0}(i)
and
y\in\mu_{0}(j)
, we have that

\displaystyle\big[\Theta_{1}(i{{}^{\prime}},j{{}^{\prime}})\circ(\lambda_{1}\times\mu_{1})^{\preccurlyeq}_{(i,j),(i{{}^{\prime}},j{{}^{\prime}})}](x,y)

\displaystyle:=\big[\Theta_{i{{}^{\prime}}}\circ\pi_{1}\big]\big(\lambda_{ii{{}^{\prime}}}^{\preccurlyeq}(x),\mu_{jj{{}^{\prime}}}^{\preccurlyeq}(y)\big)

\displaystyle:=\Theta_{i{{}^{\prime}}}\big(\lambda_{ii{{}^{\prime}}}^{\preccurlyeq}(x)\big)

\displaystyle:=\big[\big(\Theta_{i{{}^{\prime}}}\circ\lambda_{ii{{}^{\prime}}}^{\preccurlyeq}\big)\circ\pi_{1}\big](x,y)

\displaystyle:=\big(\Theta_{i}\circ\pi_{1}\big)(x,y)

\displaystyle:=\big[\Theta_{1}(i,j)\big](x,y).\qed

Proposition 5.5.15
.

If
S(\Lambda^{\preccurlyeq}):=(\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1}^{\preccurlyeq},\phi_{0}^{\Lambda^{\preccurlyeq}},\phi_{1}^{\Lambda^{\preccurlyeq}})\in\textnormal{{Spec}}(I,\preccurlyeq)

with Bishop spaces
(\mathcal{F}_{i})_{i\in I}
and Bishop morphisms
(\lambda_{ii{{}^{\prime}}})_{(i,i{{}^{\prime}})\in D^{\preccurlyeq}(I)}^{\preccurlyeq}
,
and
S(M^{\preccurlyeq}):=(\mu_{0},\mu_{1}^{\preccurlyeq},\phi_{0}^{M^{\preccurlyeq}},\phi_{1}^{M^{\preccurlyeq}})\in\textnormal{{Spec}}(J,\preccurlyeq)

with Bishop spaces
(\mathcal{G}_{j})_{j\in J}
and Bishop morphisms
(\mu_{jj{{}^{\prime}}})_{(j,j{{}^{\prime}})\in D^{\preccurlyeq}(J)}
,
there is a bijection

\theta:\underset{\to}{\mathrm{Lim}}\big(\lambda_{0}(i)\times\mu_{0}(j)\big)\to\underset{\to}{\mathrm{Lim}}\lambda_{0}(i)\times\underset{\to}{\mathrm{Lim}}\mu_{0}(j)\in\mathrm{Mor}\big(\underset{\to}{\mathrm{Lim}}(\mathcal{F}_{i}\times\mathcal{G}_{j}),\underset{\to}{\mathrm{Lim}}\mathcal{F}_{i}\times\underset{\to}{\mathrm{Lim}}\mathcal{G}_{j}\big).

Proof.

Let the operation
\theta:\underset{\to}{\mathrm{Lim}}\big(\lambda_{0}(i)\times\mu_{0}(j)\big)\rightsquigarrow\underset{\to}{\mathrm{Lim}}\lambda_{0}(i)\times\underset{\to}{\mathrm{Lim}}\mu_{0}(j)
, defined by

\theta\big(\textnormal{{eql}}_{0}^{\Lambda^{\preccurlyeq}\times M^{\preccurlyeq}}\big((i,j),(x,y)\big)\big):=\big(\textnormal{{eql}}_{0}^{\Lambda^{\preccurlyeq}}(i,x),\textnormal{{eql}}_{0}^{M^{\preccurlyeq}}(j,y)\big).

First we show that
\theta
is an embedding as follows:

\displaystyle\ \textnormal{{eql}}_{0}^{\Lambda^{\preccurlyeq}\times M^{\preccurlyeq}}\big((i,j),(x,y)\big)=\textnormal{{eql}}_{0}^{\Lambda^{\preccurlyeq}\times M^{\preccurlyeq}}\big((i{{}^{\prime}},j{{}^{\prime}}),(x{{}^{\prime}},y{{}^{\prime}})\big):\Leftrightarrow

\displaystyle:\Leftrightarrow\exists_{(k,l)\in I\times J}\big((i,j),(i{{}^{\prime}},j{{}^{\prime}})\preccurlyeq(k,l)\ \&\ (\lambda_{1}\times\mu_{1})_{(i,j)(k,l)}^{\preccurlyeq}(x,y)=(\lambda_{1}\times\mu_{1})_{(i{{}^{\prime}},j{{}^{\prime}})(k,l)}^{\preccurlyeq}(x{{}^{\prime}},y{{}^{\prime}})\big)

\displaystyle:\Leftrightarrow\exists_{(k,l)\in I\times J}\big((i,j),(i{{}^{\prime}},j{{}^{\prime}})\preccurlyeq(k,l)\ \&\ \big(\lambda_{ik}^{\preccurlyeq}(x),\mu_{jl}^{\preccurlyeq}(y)\big)=\big(\lambda_{i{{}^{\prime}}k}^{\preccurlyeq}(x{{}^{\prime}}),\mu_{j{{}^{\prime}}k}^{\preccurlyeq}(y{{}^{\prime}})\big)

\displaystyle\Leftrightarrow\exists_{k\in I}\big(i,i{{}^{\prime}}\preccurlyeq k\ \&\ \lambda_{ik}^{\preccurlyeq}(x)=\lambda_{i{{}^{\prime}}k}^{\preccurlyeq}(x{{}^{\prime}})\big)\ \&\ \exists_{l\in J}\big(j,j{{}^{\prime}}\preccurlyeq l\ \&\ \lambda_{jk}^{\preccurlyeq}(y)=\lambda_{j{{}^{\prime}}k}^{\preccurlyeq}(y{{}^{\prime}})\big)

\displaystyle:\Leftrightarrow\textnormal{{eql}}_{0}^{\Lambda^{\preccurlyeq}}(i,x)=\textnormal{{eql}}_{0}^{\Lambda^{\preccurlyeq}}(i{{}^{\prime}},x{{}^{\prime}})\ \ \&\ \ \textnormal{{eql}}_{0}^{M^{\preccurlyeq}}(j,y)=\textnormal{{eql}}_{0}^{M^{\preccurlyeq}}(j{{}^{\prime}},y{{}^{\prime}})

\displaystyle:\Leftrightarrow\big(\textnormal{{eql}}_{0}^{\Lambda^{\preccurlyeq}}(i,x),\textnormal{{eql}}_{0}^{M^{\preccurlyeq}}(j,y)\big)=\big(\textnormal{{eql}}_{0}^{\Lambda^{\preccurlyeq}}(i{{}^{\prime}},x{{}^{\prime}}),\textnormal{{eql}}_{0}^{M^{\preccurlyeq}}(j{{}^{\prime}},y{{}^{\prime}})\big)

\displaystyle:\Leftrightarrow\theta\big(\textnormal{{eql}}_{0}^{\Lambda^{\preccurlyeq}\times M^{\preccurlyeq}}\big((i,j),(x,y)\big)\big)=\theta\big(\textnormal{{eql}}_{0}^{\Lambda^{\preccurlyeq}\times M^{\preccurlyeq}}\big((i{{}^{\prime}},j{{}^{\prime}}),(x{{}^{\prime}},y{{}^{\prime}})\big)\big).

The fact that
\theta
is a surjection
is immediate to show.
By definition of the direct limit and by the
\vee
-lifting of the product Bishop topology we have that

\underset{\to}{\mathrm{Lim}}(\mathcal{F}_{i}\times\mathcal{G}_{j}):=\bigg(\underset{\to}{\mathrm{Lim}}\big(\lambda_{0}(i)\times\mu_{0}(j)\big),\bigvee_{\Xi\in\prod_{(i,j)\in I\times J}^{\succcurlyeq}F_{i}\times G_{j}}\textnormal{{eql}}_{0}f_{\Xi}\bigg),

\underset{\to}{\mathrm{Lim}}\mathcal{F}_{i}\times\underset{\to}{\mathrm{Lim}}\mathcal{G}_{j}:=\bigg(\underset{\to}{\mathrm{Lim}}\lambda_{0}(i)\times\underset{\to}{\mathrm{Lim}}\mu_{0}(j),\bigvee_{\Theta\in\prod_{i\in I}^{\succcurlyeq}F_{i}}^{H\in\prod_{j\in J}^{\succcurlyeq}G_{j}}\textnormal{{eql}}_{0}f_{\Theta}\circ\pi_{1},\textnormal{{eql}}_{0}f_{H}\circ\pi_{2}\bigg).

To show that
\theta\in\mathrm{Mor}\big(\underset{\to}{\mathrm{Lim}}(\mathcal{F}_{i}\times\mathcal{G}_{j}),\underset{\to}{\mathrm{Lim}}\mathcal{F}_{i}\times\underset{\to}{\mathrm{Lim}}\mathcal{G}_{j}\big)
it suffices to show that

\forall_{\Theta\in\prod_{i\in I}^{\succcurlyeq}F_{i}}\forall_{H\in\prod_{j\in J}^{\succcurlyeq}G_{j}}\big(\textnormal{{eql}}_{0}f_{\Theta}\circ\pi_{1})\circ\theta\in\underset{\to}{\mathrm{Lim}}(F_{i}\times G_{j})\ \&\ (\textnormal{{eql}}_{0}f_{H}\circ\pi_{2})\circ\theta\in\underset{\to}{\mathrm{Lim}}(F_{i}\times G_{j})\big).

If
\Theta\in\prod_{i\in I}^{\succcurlyeq}F_{i}
, we show that
(\textnormal{{eql}}_{0}f_{\Theta}\circ\pi_{1})\circ\theta\in\underset{\to}{\mathrm{Lim}}(F_{i}\times G_{j})

From the equalities

\displaystyle[(\textnormal{{eql}}_{0}f_{\Theta^{\succcurlyeq}}\circ\pi_{1})\circ\theta]\big(\textnormal{{eql}}_{0}^{\Lambda^{\preccurlyeq}\times M^{\preccurlyeq}}\big((i,j),(x,y)\big)\big)

\displaystyle:=(\textnormal{{eql}}_{0}f_{\Theta^{\succcurlyeq}}\circ\pi_{1})\big(\textnormal{{eql}}_{0}^{\Lambda^{\preccurlyeq}}(i,x),\textnormal{{eql}}_{0}^{M^{\preccurlyeq}}(j,y)\big)

\displaystyle:=\textnormal{{eql}}_{0}f_{\Theta^{\succcurlyeq}}\big(\textnormal{{eql}}_{0}^{\Lambda^{\preccurlyeq}}(i,x)\big)

\displaystyle:=\Theta_{i}(x)

\displaystyle:=\big(\Theta_{i}\circ\pi_{1}\big)(x,y)

\displaystyle:=\big[\Theta_{1}(i,j)\big](x,y)

\displaystyle:=\textnormal{{eql}}_{0}f_{\Theta_{1}}\big(\textnormal{{eql}}_{0}^{\Lambda^{\preccurlyeq}\times M^{\preccurlyeq}}\big((i,j),(x,y)\big),

where
\Theta_{1}\in\prod_{i\in I}^{\succcurlyeq}F_{i}\times G_{j}
is defined in Lemma
5.5.14
,
we conclude that
(\textnormal{{eql}}_{0}f_{\Theta^{\succcurlyeq}}\circ\pi_{1})\circ\theta:=\textnormal{{eql}}_{0}f_{\Theta_{1}}\in\underset{\to}{\mathrm{Lim}}(F_{i}\times G_{j})
. For the second case we work similarly.
∎

5.6
Inverse limit of a contravariant spectrum of Bishop spaces

Definition 5.6.1
.

If
S(\Lambda^{\succcurlyeq}):=(\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1}^{\succcurlyeq},\phi_{0}^{\Lambda^{\succcurlyeq}},\phi_{1}^{\Lambda^{\succcurlyeq}})

is a contravariant
(I,\preccurlyeq)
-spectrum with Bishop spaces
(\mathcal{F}_{i})_{i\in I}
and Bishop morphisms

(\lambda_{ji}^{\succcurlyeq})_{(i,j)\in D^{\preccurlyeq}(I)}
, the
inverse limit of
S\Lambda(^{\succcurlyeq})
is the Bishop space

\underset{\leftarrow}{\mathrm{Lim}}\mathcal{F}_{i}:=\big(\underset{\leftarrow}{\mathrm{Lim}}\lambda_{0}(i),\ \underset{\leftarrow}{\mathrm{Lim}}F_{i}\big),

\underset{\leftarrow}{\mathrm{Lim}}\lambda_{0}(i):=\prod_{i\in I}^{\succcurlyeq}\lambda_{0}(i)\ \ \ \&\ \ \ \ \underset{\leftarrow}{\mathrm{Lim}}F_{i}:=\bigvee_{i\in I}^{f\in F_{i}}f\circ\pi_{i}^{\Lambda^{\succcurlyeq}}.

For simplicity we write
\pi_{i}
instead of
\pi_{i}^{\Lambda^{\succcurlyeq}}
for the
function
\pi_{i}^{\Lambda^{\succcurlyeq}}:\prod_{i\in I}^{\succcurlyeq}\lambda_{0}(i)\to\lambda_{0}(i)
, which is
defined, as its dual
\pi_{i}^{\Lambda^{\preccurlyeq}}
in the Proposition
3.8.4
(iv), by
the rule
\Phi\mapsto\Phi_{i}
, for every
i\in I
..

Proposition 5.6.2

(Universal property of the inverse limit)
.

If
S(\Lambda^{\succcurlyeq}):=(\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1}^{\succcurlyeq},\phi_{0}^{\Lambda^{\succcurlyeq}},\phi_{1}^{\Lambda^{\succcurlyeq}})

is a contravariant direct spectrum over
(I,\preccurlyeq)
with Bishop spaces
(\mathcal{F}_{i})_{i\in I}
and
Bishop morphisms
(\lambda_{ji}^{\succcurlyeq})_{(i,j)\in\preccurlyeq(I)}
, its inverse limit
\underset{\leftarrow}{\mathrm{Lim}}\mathcal{F}_{i}

satisfies the universal property of inverse limits i.e.,

(i)

For every
i\in I
, we have that
\pi_{i}\in\mathrm{Mor}(\underset{\leftarrow}{\mathrm{Lim}}\mathcal{F}_{i},\mathcal{F}_{i})
.

(ii)

If
i\preccurlyeq j
, the following left diagram commutes
\prod_{i\in I}^{\succcurlyeq}\lambda_{0}(i)
\lambda_{0}(j)
\lambda_{0}(i)
\lambda_{0}(i)
\lambda_{0}(j)
.
Y
\ \ \pi_{j}
\pi_{i}\
\lambda_{ji}^{\succcurlyeq}
\ \varpi_{j}
\varpi_{i}\
\lambda_{ji}^{\preccurlyeq}

(iii)

If
\mathcal{G}:=(Y,G)
is a Bishop space and

\varpi_{i}:Y\to\lambda_{0}(i)\in\mathrm{Mor}(\mathcal{G},\mathcal{F}_{i})
, for every
i\in I
, such that if

i\preccurlyeq j
,
the above right diagram commutes, there is a
unique function
h:Y\to\prod_{i\in I}\lambda_{0}(i)\in\mathrm{Mor}(\mathcal{G},\underset{\leftarrow}{\mathrm{Lim}}\mathcal{F}_{i})

such that the following diagrams commute
Y
\lambda_{0}(j)
,
\lambda_{0}(i)
\prod_{i\in I}\lambda_{0}(i)
\ \ \varpi_{j}
\varpi_{i}\
\lambda_{ji}^{\succcurlyeq}\ \ \ \ \ \
\ \ \pi_{j}
\pi_{i}\
\ \ \ h

Proof.

The condition

\pi_{i}\in\mathrm{Mor}(\underset{\leftarrow}{\mathrm{Lim}}\mathcal{F}_{i},\mathcal{F}_{i}):\Leftrightarrow\forall_{f\in F_{i}}\bigg(f\circ\pi_{i}\in\bigvee_{i\in I}^{f\in F_{i}}f\circ\pi_{i}\bigg)
is trivially satisfied, and (i) follows.
For (ii), the required equality
\lambda_{ji}^{\succcurlyeq}\big(\pi_{j}(\Phi)\big)=_{{\lambda_{0}(i)}}\pi_{i}(\Phi):\Leftrightarrow\lambda_{ji}^{\succcurlyeq}(\Phi_{j})=_{{\lambda_{0}(i)}}\Phi_{i}
holds by
the definition of
\prod_{i\in I}^{\succcurlyeq}\lambda_{0}(i)
.
To show (iii), let the operation
h:Y\rightsquigarrow\prod_{i\in I}^{\succcurlyeq}\lambda_{0}(i)
, defined by

h(y):=\Phi_{y}
, where
\Phi_{y}(i):=\varpi_{i}(y)
, for every
y\in Y
and
i\in I
.
First we show that
h
is well-defined i.e.,
h(y)\in\prod_{i\in I}^{\succcurlyeq}\lambda_{0}(i)
. If
i\preccurlyeq j
, by the
supposed commutativity of the above right diagram we have that

\lambda_{ji}^{\succcurlyeq}\big(\Phi_{y}(j)\big):=\lambda_{ji}^{\succcurlyeq}\big(\varpi_{j}(y)\big)=\varpi_{i}(y):=\Phi_{y}(i)
.
Next we show that
h
is a function.
If
y=_{Y}y{{}^{\prime}}
,
the last formula in the following equivalences

\Phi_{y}=_{{\prod_{i\in I}^{\succcurlyeq}\lambda_{0}(i)}}\Phi_{y{{}^{\prime}}}:\Leftrightarrow\forall_{i\in I}\big(\Phi_{y}(i)=_{{\lambda_{0}(i)}}\Phi_{y{{}^{\prime}}}(i)\big):\Leftrightarrow\forall_{i\in I}\big(\varpi_{i}(y)=_{{\lambda_{0}(i)}}\varpi_{i}(y{{}^{\prime}})\big)

holds by the fact that
\varpi_{i}
is a function, for every
i\in I
. By the

\bigvee
-lifting of morphisms we have that

h\in\mathrm{Mor}(\mathcal{G},\underset{\leftarrow}{\mathrm{Lim}}\mathcal{F}_{i})\Leftrightarrow\forall_{i\in I}\forall_{f\in F_{i}}\big((f\circ\pi_{i})\circ h\in G\big)
.
If
i\in I
,
f\in F_{i}
, and
y\in Y
, then

\big[(f\circ\pi_{i})\circ h\big](y):=(f\circ\pi_{i})(\Phi_{y}):=f\big(\Phi_{y}(i)\big):=f\big(\varpi_{i}(y)\big):=(f\circ\varpi_{i})(y),

hence
(f\circ\pi_{i})\circ h:=f\circ\varpi_{i}\in G
, since
\varpi_{i}\in\mathrm{Mor}(\mathcal{G},\mathcal{F}_{i})
. The required
commutativity of the last diagram above, and the uniqueness of
h
follow immediately.
∎

The uniqueness of
\underset{\leftarrow}{\mathrm{Lim}}\lambda_{0}(i)
, up to Bishop isomorphism, follows easily from
its universal property. Next follows the inverse analogue to the Theorem
5.5.8
.

Theorem 5.6.3
.

Let
S(\Lambda^{\succcurlyeq}):=(\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1}^{\succcurlyeq},\phi_{0}^{\Lambda^{\succcurlyeq}},\phi_{1}^{\Lambda^{\succcurlyeq}})
be
a contravariant
(I,\preccurlyeq)
-spectrum with Bishop spaces
(\mathcal{F}_{i})_{i\in I}
and Bishop morphisms

(\lambda_{ji}^{\succcurlyeq})_{(i,j)\in D^{\preccurlyeq}(I)}
,
S(M^{\succcurlyeq}):=(\mu_{0},\mu_{1},\phi_{0}^{M^{\succcurlyeq}},\phi_{1}^{M^{\succcurlyeq}})

a contravariant
(I,\preccurlyeq)
-spectrum with Bishop spaces
(\mathcal{G}_{i})_{i\in I}
and Bishop morphisms

(\mu_{ji}^{\succcurlyeq})_{(i,j)\in D^{\preccurlyeq}(I)}
, and
\Psi\colon S(\Lambda^{\succcurlyeq})\Rightarrow S(M^{\succcurlyeq})
.

(i)

There is a unique function
\Psi_{\leftarrow}\colon\underset{\leftarrow}{\mathrm{Lim}}\lambda_{0}(i)\to\underset{\leftarrow}{\mathrm{Lim}}\mu_{0}(i)
such that, for every
i\in I
, the following diagram commutes
\underset{\leftarrow}{\mathrm{Lim}}\lambda_{0}(i)
\underset{\leftarrow}{\mathrm{Lim}}\mu_{0}(i)
.
\mu_{0}(i)
\lambda_{0}(i)
\Psi_{\leftarrow}
\Psi_{i}
\pi^{\Lambda^{\succcurlyeq}}_{i}
\pi^{M^{\succcurlyeq}}_{i}

(ii)

If
\Psi
is continuous, then

\Psi_{\leftarrow}\in\mathrm{Mor}(\underset{\leftarrow}{\mathrm{Lim}}\mathcal{F}_{i},\underset{\leftarrow}{\mathrm{Lim}}\mathcal{G}_{i})
.

(iii)

If
\Psi_{i}
is an embedding, for every
i\in I
,
then
\Psi_{\leftarrow}
is an embedding.

Proof.

(i) Let the assignment routine
\Psi_{\leftarrow}\colon\underset{\leftarrow}{\mathrm{Lim}}\lambda_{0}(i)\rightsquigarrow\underset{\leftarrow}{\mathrm{Lim}}\mu_{0}(i)
, defined by

\Theta\mapsto\Psi_{\leftarrow}(\Theta),\ \ \ \ \big[\Psi_{\leftarrow}(\Theta)\big]_{i}:=\Psi_{i}(\Theta_{i});\ \ \ \ \Theta\in\underset{\leftarrow}{\mathrm{Lim}}\lambda_{0}(i),\ i\in I.

First we show that
\Psi_{\leftarrow}
is well-defined i.e.,
\Psi_{\leftarrow}(\Theta)\in\prod_{i\in I}^{\succcurlyeq}\mu_{0}(i).

If
i\preccurlyeq j
, since
\Theta\in\prod_{i\in I}^{\succcurlyeq}\lambda_{0}(i)
, we have that

\Theta_{i}=\lambda_{ji}^{\succcurlyeq}(\Theta_{j})
, and since
\Psi\colon S(\Lambda^{\succcurlyeq})\Rightarrow S(M^{\succcurlyeq})
\mu_{0}(i)
\mu_{0}(j)
,
\lambda_{0}(i)
\lambda_{0}(j)
\mu_{ji}^{\succcurlyeq}
\lambda_{ji}^{\succcurlyeq}
\Psi_{i}
\Psi_{j}

\big[\Psi_{\leftarrow}(\Theta)\big]_{i}:=\Psi_{i}(\Theta_{i})=\Psi_{i}\big(\lambda_{ji}^{\succcurlyeq}(\Theta_{j})\big):=\big(\Psi_{i}\circ\lambda_{ji}^{\succcurlyeq}\big)(\Theta_{j})=\big(\mu_{ji}^{\succcurlyeq}\circ\Psi_{j}\big)(\Theta_{j}):=\mu_{ji}^{\succcurlyeq}\big(\Psi_{j}(\Theta_{j})\big):=\mu_{ji}^{\succcurlyeq}\big(\big[\Psi_{\leftarrow}(\Theta)\big]_{j}\big).

Next we show that
\Psi_{\leftarrow}
is a function:

\Theta=_{{\underset{\leftarrow}{\mathrm{Lim}}\lambda_{0}(i)}}\Phi:\Leftrightarrow\forall_{i\in I}\big(\Theta_{i}=_{{\lambda_{0}(i)}}\Phi_{i}\big)\Rightarrow\forall_{i\in I}\big(\Psi_{i}(\Theta_{i})=_{{\mu_{0}(i)}}\Psi_{i}(\Phi_{i})\big):\Leftrightarrow\forall_{i\in I}\big(\big[\Psi_{\leftarrow}(\Theta)\big]_{i}=_{{\mu_{0}(i)}}\big[\Psi_{\leftarrow}(\Phi)\big]_{i}\big):\Leftrightarrow\Psi_{\leftarrow}(\Theta)=_{{\underset{\leftarrow}{\mathrm{Lim}}\mu_{0}(i)}}\Psi_{\leftarrow}(\Phi)
.
The commutativity of the diagram and the uniqueness of
\Psi_{\leftarrow}
are immediate to show.

(ii) By the
\bigvee
-lifting of morphisms we have that

\Psi_{\leftarrow}\in\mathrm{Mor}(\underset{\leftarrow}{\mathrm{Lim}}\mathcal{F}_{i},\underset{\leftarrow}{\mathrm{Lim}}\mathcal{G}_{i})\Leftrightarrow\forall_{i\in I}\forall_{g\in G_{i}}\big((g\circ\pi_{i}^{M^{\succcurlyeq}})\circ\Psi_{\leftarrow}\in\underset{\leftarrow}{\mathrm{Lim}}F_{i}\big).

If
i\in I
and
g\in G_{i}
, then

[(g\circ\pi_{i}^{M^{\succcurlyeq}})\circ\Psi_{\leftarrow}](\Theta):=g\big([\Psi_{\leftarrow}(\Theta)\big]_{i}\big):=g\big(\Psi_{i}(\Theta_{i})\big):=\big(g\circ\Psi_{i}\big)(\Theta_{i}):=\big[\big(g\circ\Psi_{i}\big)\circ\pi_{i}^{\Lambda^{\succcurlyeq}}\big](\Theta),

and
g\circ\Psi_{i}\in F_{i}
, by the continuity of
\Psi
, hence

(g\circ\pi_{i}^{M^{\succcurlyeq}})\circ\Psi_{\leftarrow}:=\big(g\circ\Psi_{i}\big)\circ\pi_{i}^{\Lambda^{\succcurlyeq}}\in\underset{\leftarrow}{\mathrm{Lim}}F_{i}
.

(iii) By definition we have that

\Psi_{\leftarrow}(\Theta)=_{{\underset{\leftarrow}{\mathrm{Lim}}\mu_{0}(i)}}\Psi_{\leftarrow}(\Phi):\Leftrightarrow\forall_{i\in I}\big(\Psi_{i}(\Theta_{i})=_{{\mu_{0}(i)}}\Psi_{i}(\Phi_{i})\big)\Rightarrow

\forall_{i\in I}\big(\Theta_{i}=_{{\lambda_{0}(i)}}\Phi_{i}\big):\Leftrightarrow\Theta=_{{\underset{\leftarrow}{\mathrm{Lim}}\lambda_{0}(i)}}\Phi.

∎

Proposition 5.6.4
.

If
S(\Lambda^{\succcurlyeq}):=(\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1}^{\succcurlyeq},\phi_{0}^{\Lambda^{\succcurlyeq}},\phi_{1}^{\Lambda^{\succcurlyeq}})

The required equality is reduced to

\forall_{i\in I}\big(\big[(\Xi\circ\Psi)_{\leftarrow}(\Theta)\big]_{i}=_{{\nu_{0}(i)}}\big[\Xi_{\leftarrow}(\Psi_{\leftarrow}(\Theta))\big]_{i}\big)
.

, then
